{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}
.
Because of our assumption that
f_{0}\in{\mathcal{F}}
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
is a subset of
C([0,1]^{m})
as well.
Also, since
(C([0,1]^{m}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
is a separable metric space,
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
is also separable with respect to the sup-norm.
Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{0}
be a countable dense subset of
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
with respect to the sup-norm.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{g\in{\mathcal{G}}_{0}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}g(X^{(i)})\Big|,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{g\in{\mathcal{G}}_{0}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}g(X^{(i)})\Big|,

and

\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\Big|=\sup_{g\in{\mathcal{G}}_{0}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}g(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\Big|

so that all the supremums are measurable.
For these reasons, in this case, Theorem
E.12
is valid without the countability assumption.

Remark E.14

(Well-specified Case)
.

If
f^{*}
belongs to the function class
{\mathcal{F}}
, we can set
f_{0}=f^{*}
.
In this case, the conditions
t_{n}\geq 4\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{P,2}
and

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}

always hold, and (
68
) is simplified as

{\mathbb{P}}(\|\hat{f}-f^{*}\|_{P,2}>t)\leq\epsilon+C\cdot\frac{1+M^{3}}{t^{3}}\cdot t_{n}^{3}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+M^{3}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}t^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3}n^{\frac{3}{q}}+M^{3})}{n^{3}t^{6}}.

This simplified version will be used for our proof of Theorem
3.5
.

Recall that our estimator
\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}
is defined as a least squares estimator over

\big\{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}:V_{\text{mars}}(f)\leq V\big\},

which is not uniformly bounded as required for
{\mathcal{F}}
in Theorem
E.12
.
Theorem
E.12
is therefore not directly applicable to our estimator, but we can avoid this problem by using the fact that
\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}
is (uniformly) bounded in probability.
Due to the boundedness of
\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}
as stated in Lemma
E.15
(proved in Appendix
E.7.5
), it is guaranteed that
\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}
is a least squares estimator over a uniformly bounded function class with high probability.

Lemma E.15
.

The estimator
\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}
satisfies that

\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(1).

We also use the following theorem from the same paper (
Han and Wellner 2019
).
This theorem will help us bound

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

in Theorem
E.12
using upper bounds of

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq rt_{k}\end{subarray}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

for suitably chosen
t_{k}
, for
1\leq k\leq n
.

Theorem E.16
(
Han and Wellner 2019
, Corollary 1).

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
be countable collections of real-valued functions defined on
\mathcal{X}
for which
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}\supseteq{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
for every
1\leq k\leq n
.
Suppose
X^{(1)},\dots,X^{(n)}
are permutation invariant random variables on
\mathcal{X}
and
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}
are i.i.d. mean zero random variables independent of
X^{(1)},\dots,X^{(n)}
.
Assume that there exist positive constants
r\geq 1
and
C
such that

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq C\cdot k^{\frac{1}{r}}

for every
1\leq k\leq n
.
Then, for every
q\geq 1
, we have

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{n}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq 4C\cdot\|\xi_{1}\|_{\min\{q,r\},1}\cdot n^{1/\min\{q,r\}},

where

\|\xi_{1}\|_{p,1}:=\int_{0}^{\infty}({\mathbb{P}}(|\xi_{1}|>t))^{\frac{1}{p}}\,dt

for each
p\geq 1
.

As in Theorem
E.12
, the countability assumption in Theorem
E.16
can be dropped if
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}
are subsets of
C([0,1]^{m})
.

Suppose we are given
\epsilon>0
.
By Lemma
E.15
, there exists
M>0
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\big)\geq 1-\epsilon

for sufficiently large
n
.
For
V>0
, let

{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)=\big\{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}:V_{\text{mars}}(f)\leq V\mbox{ and }\|f-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\big\}.

Then, it is clear from the definition of
\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}
that for sufficiently large
n
,

\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}\in\mathop{\rm argmin}_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(y_{i}-f(X^{(i)})\big)^{2}\bigg\}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
.
Also, we can see that Theorem
E.12
is valid for
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)
since
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)
is a subset of
C([0,1]^{d})
.

We first bound

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

for
1\leq k\leq n
and
t>0
.
The following lemma (proved in Appendix
E.7.6
) enables us to bound it using the bracketing entropy integral of
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}
(under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}
).
Recall that the bracketing entropy integral of a function class
{\mathcal{G}}
under a norm
\|\cdot\|
is defined as

J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|)=\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{1+\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{G}},\|\cdot\|)}d\epsilon

for
t>0
.

Lemma E.17
.

There exists a universal positive constant
C
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq CJ_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\cdot\bigg(1+M\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}{t^{2}\sqrt{k}}\bigg)

for
t>0
.

Also, the following lemma provides an upper bound of the bracketing entropy of
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}
(under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}
). We defer our proof of this lemma to Appendix
E.7.7
.

Lemma E.18
.

There exist positive constants
C_{s}
depending on
s
and
C_{B,d}
depending on
B
and
d
such that

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq 2^{d}\log\Big(2+C_{s}\cdot\frac{M+V}{\epsilon}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{B,d}\Big(2^{d+2}+\frac{2^{d+3}V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg[\log\Big(2^{d+2}+\frac{2^{d+3}V}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{2(s-1)}

for every
V>0,M>0
, and
\epsilon>0
.

By Lemma
E.18
and the inequality
(x+y)^{1/2}\leq x^{1/2}+y^{1/2}
,

\displaystyle J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-

\displaystyle\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})=\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{1+\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq t

\displaystyle+2^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{\log\Big(2+C_{s}\cdot\frac{M+V}{\epsilon}\Big)}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle+C_{B,d}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(2^{d+2}+\frac{2^{d+3}V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg[\log\Big(2^{d+2}+\frac{2^{d+3}V}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{s-1}\,d\epsilon.

We use Lemma
E.7
again for bounding the above integrals.
Lemma
E.7
implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{\log\Big(2+C_{s}\cdot\frac{M+V}{\epsilon}\Big)}\,d\epsilon\leq\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{\log\Big(\frac{2t+C_{s}(M+V)}{\epsilon}\Big)}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\qquad\leq Ct\bigg[1+2\log\Big(2+C_{s}\cdot\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)\bigg]\leq C_{s}t+Ct\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)

and that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big(2^{d+2}+\frac{2^{d+3}V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg[\log\Big(2^{d+2}+\frac{2^{d+3}V}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{s-1}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{2^{d+2}(t+2V)}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{2^{d+2}(t+2V)}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{s-1}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C_{s}\big[2^{d+2}(t+2V)\big]^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}\bigg[1+\Big\{\log\Big(2^{d+2}+\frac{2^{d+3}V}{t}\Big)\Big\}^{s-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C_{d}(t+V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}})\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C_{d}t\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}+C_{d}V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}.

Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq C_{d}t\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)+C_{B,d}t\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+C_{B,d}V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}.

Note that if
t\leq V
,

C_{B,d}t\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}\leq C_{B,d}V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}

and otherwise,

C_{B,d}t\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}\leq C_{B,d}t\leq C_{B,d}t\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big).

Therefore, we have

J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq C_{B,d}t\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)+C_{B,d}V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1},

which leads to that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f(X_{i})\Big|\bigg]\leq C_{B,d}t\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+C_{B,d}V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}+C_{B,d}Mk^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+C_{B,d}MV^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{-\frac{1}{4}}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+C_{B,d}MV^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2(s-1)}:=\Psi_{k}(t)

due to Lemma
E.17
.

Note that
t\rightarrow\Psi_{k}(t)/t
is monotonically decreasing.
Also, observe that for

t\geq(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})k^{-\frac{1}{2}},

we have

C_{B,d}t\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{k}t^{2}\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{(M+V)k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big),

\displaystyle C_{B,d}Mk^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{k}t^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}M^{\frac{1}{2}}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{(M+V)k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big),

C_{B,d}V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{k}t^{2}\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}V^{\frac{1}{5}}k^{-\frac{2}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vk^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}},

\displaystyle C_{B,d}MV^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{-\frac{1}{4}}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{M+V}{t}\Big)\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{k}t^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}M^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}k^{-\frac{4}{9}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{(M+V)k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4}{9}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vk^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{9}},

and

\displaystyle C_{B,d}MV^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2(s-1)}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{k}t^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}M^{\frac{2}{5}}V^{\frac{1}{5}}k^{-\frac{2}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vk^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}.

Clearly, there exist some positive constants
C_{s}
and
C
such that

[\log(2+x)]^{\frac{4s}{9}}\leq x^{\frac{4}{45}}\qquad\text{for $x\geq C_{s}$}

and

\log(2+x)\leq x^{\frac{1}{5}}\qquad\text{for $x\geq C$}.

Thus, it follows that

\displaystyle C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{(M+V)k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})k^{-\frac{1}{2}}+C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})k^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{(M+V)k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{5}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})k^{-\frac{1}{2}}+C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{4}{5}}(M+V)^{\frac{1}{5}}k^{-\frac{2}{5}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{4}{5}}\big[(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{1}{5}}+(M+V)^{\frac{1}{5}}\big]k^{-\frac{2}{5}}

and

\displaystyle C_{B,d}M^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}k^{-\frac{4}{9}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{(M+V)k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4}{9}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vk^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{9}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C_{B,d}M^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}k^{-\frac{4}{9}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{(M+V)k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4s}{9}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C_{B,d}M^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}k^{-\frac{4}{9}}+C_{B,d}M^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}k^{-\frac{4}{9}}\Big(\frac{(M+V)k^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)^{\frac{4}{45}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C_{B,d}M^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}\bigg[1+\Big(\frac{M+V}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)^{\frac{4}{45}}\bigg]k^{-\frac{2}{5}}.

For these reasons, for
k=1,\dots,n
, if we let

\displaystyle t_{k}=\bigg\{C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{4}{5}}\big[(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{1}{5}}+(M+V)^{\frac{1}{5}}\big]+C_{B,d}M^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}\bigg[1+\Big(\frac{M+V}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)^{\frac{4}{45}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{4}{5}}V^{\frac{1}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vn^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\bigg\}\cdot k^{-\frac{2}{5}}

then

\Psi_{k}(t_{k})\leq\sqrt{k}t_{k}^{2}.

Hence, for
r\geq 1
, we have

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{k}\end{subarray}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq\sqrt{k}\cdot\Psi_{k}(rt_{k})\leq r\sqrt{k}\cdot\Psi_{k}(t_{k})\leq rkt_{k}^{2},

where the second inequality follows from that
t\rightarrow\Psi_{k}(t)/t
is monotonically decreasing.

Recall that we assume
\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1}<\infty
.
Using Theorem
E.16
, we can thus show that

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq 4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1}\cdot rnt_{n}^{2}

for
r\geq 1
.
Therefore, if we redefine
t_{n}
as
(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})t_{n}
, then

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}

and

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}.

As a result, by Theorem
E.12
(and Remark
E.14
),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>t\big)\leq\epsilon

\displaystyle+C\cdot\frac{1+M^{3}}{t^{3}}\cdot t_{n}^{3}

\displaystyle+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+M^{3}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}t^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{5}^{3}n^{\frac{3}{5}}+M^{3})}{n^{3}t^{6}}

provided that
n
is sufficiently large and
t\geq t_{n}
.

Observe that if
s=1
,

\displaystyle t_{n}

\displaystyle\leq(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\cdot\bigg\{C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{4}{5}}\big[(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{1}{5}}+(M+V)^{\frac{1}{5}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+C_{B,d}M^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}\bigg[1+\Big(\frac{M+V}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)^{\frac{4}{45}}\bigg]\bigg\}\cdot n^{-\frac{2}{5}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\cdot C_{B,d,V,M}\cdot n^{-\frac{2}{5}},

and otherwise (
s\geq 2
),

\displaystyle t_{n}

\displaystyle=(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\cdot C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{4}{5}}V^{\frac{1}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vn^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\cdot n^{-\frac{2}{5}}+O(n^{-\frac{2}{5}})

\displaystyle\leq(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\cdot C_{B,d,V,M}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vn^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\cdot n^{-\frac{2}{5}}+O(n^{-\frac{2}{5}}),

where the multiplicative constant underlying
O(\cdot)
depends on
B,d,V,M
, and the moments of
\xi_{i}
.
Thus, if

K\geq 2(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\cdot C_{B,d,V,M},

then

Kn^{-\frac{2}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\geq t_{n}

for sufficiently large
n
.
Hence, for such
K
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>Kn^{-\frac{2}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\epsilon+\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bigg[C\cdot\frac{(1+M^{3})t_{n}^{3}}{K^{3}n^{-\frac{6}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{12(s-1)}{5}}}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+M^{3}}{K^{3}n^{\frac{3}{10}}(\log n)^{\frac{12(s-1)}{5}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{5}^{3}n^{\frac{3}{5}}+M^{3})}{K^{6}n^{\frac{3}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{24(s-1)}{5}}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\epsilon+(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})^{3}\cdot C_{B,d,V,M}\cdot\frac{1}{K^{3}}+\|\xi_{1}\|_{5}^{3}\cdot CM^{3}\cdot\frac{1}{K^{6}}.

Consequently, we can find
K>0
for which

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>Kn^{-\frac{2}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\big)<2\epsilon

for sufficiently large
n
.
∎

E.3.5
Proof of Theorem
3.6

We prove Theorem
3.6
by using
Gao 2013
, Theorem 1.1
and ideas in the proof of
Theorem 1.2 of the same paper.
For a positive integer
m
and
S>0
, let
{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}(S)
be the collection of all the functions on
[0,1]^{m}
of the form

(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\mapsto\int\mathbf{1}\{x_{1}\geq t_{1}\}\cdots\mathbf{1}\{x_{m}\geq t_{m}\}\,d\nu(t)=\nu([\mathbf{0},x])

where
\nu
is a positive measure on
[0,1]^{m}
with
\nu([0,1]^{m})\leq S
.

Gao 2013
, Theorem 1.1 (restated below) provides an upper bound of the bracketing entropy of
{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}(S)
under the
L^{p}
norm for each
p\geq 1
.
Also, ideas in the proof of
Gao 2013
, Theorem 1.2 enable us to obtain an upper bound of the bracketing entropy of
{\mathcal{D}}_{m}
under the
L^{2}
norm from those of
{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}(S)
under the
L^{1}
and
L^{2}
norms.

Theorem E.19
(
Gao 2013
, Theorem 1.1).

Suppose that
p\geq 1
and
m
is a positive integer. Then, there exists a positive constant
C_{m,p}
depending on
m
and
p
such that

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}(S),\|\cdot\|_{p})\leq C_{m,p}\cdot\frac{S}{\epsilon}\Big|\log\frac{S}{\epsilon}\Big|^{2(m-1)}

for every
\epsilon>0
.

Assume that
0<\epsilon<2
.
If
\epsilon\geq 2
, then

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{D}}_{m},\|\cdot\|_{2})=0

because

-1\leq\int(x_{1}-t_{1})_{+}\cdots(x_{m}-t_{m})_{+}\,d\nu(t)\leq 1

for every signed measure
\nu
on
[0,1]^{m}
with variation
|\nu|([0,1])^{m}\leq 1
.
Denote by
{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+}
the subcollection of
{\mathcal{D}}_{m}
consisting of all the functions on
[0,1]^{m}
of the form

\int(x_{1}-t_{1})_{+}\cdots(x_{m}-t_{m})_{+}\,d\nu(t)

where
\nu
is a positive measure on
[0,1]^{m}
with
\nu([0,1]^{m})\leq 1
.
Then, since

{\mathcal{D}}_{m}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+}-{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+},

it follows that

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{D}}_{m},\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq 2\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{2},{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big).

(69)

Recall that we denote by
\mathcal{T}_{m}:L^{2}([0,1]^{m})\rightarrow C([0,1]^{m})
the
m
-dimensional integral operator defined by

\mathcal{T}_{m}f(x)=\int_{[\mathbf{0},x]}f(s)\,ds\mbox{\quad for }x\in[0,1]^{m}.

Since

\int_{[0,1]^{m}}(x_{1}-t_{1})_{+}\cdots(x_{m}-t_{m})_{+}\,d\nu(t)=\int_{[0,x]}\nu([\mathbf{0},s])\,ds\mbox{\quad for }x\in[0,1]^{m}

for every positive measure
\nu
on
[0,1]^{m}
with
\nu([0,1]^{m})\leq 1
, we have

{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+}=\mathcal{T}_{m}({\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+})

where
{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}:={\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}(1)
.

Now, fix
\delta>0
, which will be specified later, and let
K=N(\delta,{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+},\|\cdot\|_{1})
.
Note that by Theorem
E.19
,

\log K=\log N(\delta,{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+},\|\cdot\|_{1})\leq\log N_{[\ ]}(\delta,{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+},\|\cdot\|_{1})\leq C_{m}\cdot\frac{1}{\delta}\Big|\log\frac{1}{\delta}\Big|^{2(m-1)}.

(70)

Also, let
h_{1},\dots,h_{K}
be a
\delta
-net of
H_{m}^{+}
with respect to the
L^{1}
norm, i.e.,

{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}\subseteq\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}B_{{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}}(\delta,h_{k},\|\cdot\|_{1})

where
B_{{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}}(\delta,h_{k},\|\cdot\|_{1}):=\{h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}:\|h-h_{k}\|_{1}\leq\delta\}
.
Next, for
k=1,\dots,K
, let

{\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{+}=\Big\{\mathcal{T}_{m}((h-h_{k})_{+}):h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}\ \mbox{ and }\ \|h-h_{k}\|_{1}\leq\delta\Big\}

and

{\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{-}=\Big\{\mathcal{T}_{m}((h_{k}-h)_{+}):h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}\ \mbox{ and }\ \|h-h_{k}\|_{1}\leq\delta\Big\}.

Observe that
{\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{+},{\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{-}\subseteq{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}(\delta)
.
Indeed, if
h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}
and
\|h-h_{k}\|_{1}\leq\delta
, then for a positive measure
\nu
on
[0,1]^{m}
defined by

d\nu(t)=(h(t)-h_{k}(t))_{+}\,dt,

we have

\nu([0,1]^{m})=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}(h(t)-h_{k}(t))_{+}\,dt\leq\|h-h_{k}\|_{1}\leq\delta

and

\mathcal{T}_{m}((h-h_{k})_{+})(x)=\int_{[\mathbf{0},x]}(h(s)-h_{k}(s))_{+}\,ds=\int_{[\mathbf{0},x]}\,d\nu(s)=\nu([\mathbf{0},x])

for all
x\in[0,1]^{m}
.
Also, because
{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+}=\mathcal{T}_{m}({\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+})
,
h_{1},\dots,h_{K}
form a
\delta
-net of
H_{m}^{+}
with respect to the
L^{1}
norm, and

\mathcal{T}_{m}(h)=\mathcal{T}_{m}((h-h_{k})_{+}-(h_{k}-h)_{+}+h_{k})=\mathcal{T}_{m}((h-h_{k})_{+})-\mathcal{T}_{m}((h_{k}-h)_{+})+\mathcal{T}_{m}(h_{k})

for
h\in{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}
and
k=1,\dots,K
, it follows that

{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+}\subseteq\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}\Big({\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{+}-{\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{-}+\{\mathcal{T}_{m}(h_{k})\}\Big).

Thus,

N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+},\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{2},{\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{+},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)\cdot N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{2},{\mathcal{I}}_{k}^{-},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big),

which together with (
70
) and Theorem
E.19
implies that

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+},\|\cdot\|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\log K+2\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{2},{\mathcal{H}}_{m}^{+}(\delta),\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m}\cdot\frac{1}{\delta}\Big|\log\frac{1}{\delta}\Big|^{2(m-1)}+C_{m}\cdot\frac{2\delta}{\epsilon}\Big|\log\frac{2\delta}{\epsilon}\Big|^{2(m-1)}.

Therefore, with the choice of
\delta=(\epsilon/2)^{1/2}
, we can derive that

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{D}}_{m}^{+},\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq C_{m}\cdot\Big(\frac{2}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big|\log\frac{2}{\epsilon}\Big|^{2(m-1)}.

By (
69
), we can conclude that

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{D}}_{m},\|\cdot\|_{2})\leq C_{m}\cdot\Big(\frac{4}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big|\log\frac{4}{\epsilon}\Big|^{2(m-1)}.

∎

E.3.6
Proof of Theorem
3.8

Suppose we are given
\epsilon>0
.
By repeating our argument in Lemma
E.15
, we can show that
there exists
M>0
independent of
N
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\tilde{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq M\big)\geq 1-\epsilon

for sufficiently large
n
.
Recall that we let
{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx}}(V)
be the collection of all the functions
f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}
with
V_{\text{mars}}(f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}})\leq V
where
\nu_{\alpha}
is concentrated on
(\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{k})\cap[0,1)^{|\alpha|}
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
.
Due to Lemma
E.11
, there exists
f_{0}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx}}(V)
such that

\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2V}{N}\ \mbox{ and }\ \|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}\leq C_{d}\Big(\frac{B}{N^{3}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}V.

Thus, by the triangle inequality, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\|\tilde{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq M+\frac{2V}{N}\Big)\geq 1-\epsilon

for sufficiently large
n
.
For
V>0
, let

{\mathcal{F}}_{M+2V/N}(V)=\Big\{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}:V_{\text{mars}}(f)\leq V\mbox{ and }\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq M+\frac{2V}{N}\Big\}

as in the proof of Theorem
3.5
,
and let

{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},M+2V/N}(V)=\Big\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx}}(V):\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq M+\frac{2V}{N}\Big\}.

Then, from the definition of
\tilde{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}
, we can see that

\tilde{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}\in\mathop{\rm argmin}_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},M+2V/N}(V)}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(y_{i}-f(x^{(i)})\big)^{2}\bigg\}.

with probability at least
1-\epsilon
.

Through the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
3.5
with replacing
f^{*}
with
f_{0}
and
M
with
\tilde{M}:=M+2V/N
, we can show that for

\displaystyle t_{n}=(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\bigg\{C_{B,d}(1+\tilde{M}^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{4}{5}}\big[(1+\tilde{M}^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{1}{5}}+(\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{5}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{B,d}\tilde{M}^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}\bigg[1+\Big(\frac{\tilde{M}+V}{1+\tilde{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)^{\frac{4}{45}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{B,d}(1+\tilde{M}^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{4}{5}}V^{\frac{1}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vn^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+\tilde{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\bigg\}\cdot n^{-\frac{2}{5}},

we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}

and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq r\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}.

Hence, to apply Theorem
E.12
, it suffices to find an upper bound of

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

for
t>0
.

First, by making minor modifications to the proof of Lemma
E.17
, we can prove that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\cdot J_{[\ ]}\big(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\bigg(1+\tilde{M}\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}\big(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}\big)}{t^{2}\sqrt{n}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\cdot\frac{V}{N}\cdot J_{[\ ]}\big(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg(1+\tilde{M}\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}\big(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}\big)}{t^{2}\sqrt{n}}\bigg).

Next, repeating the same computation as in the proof of Theorem
3.5
, we can derive that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq\frac{V}{N}\bigg\{C_{B,d}t\log\Big(2+\frac{\tilde{M}+V}{t}\Big)+C_{B,d}V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{B,d}\tilde{M}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{\tilde{M}+V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{B,d}\tilde{M}V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{-\frac{1}{4}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{\tilde{M}+V}{t}\Big)\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{B,d}\tilde{M}V^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2(s-1)}\bigg\}:=\tilde{\Psi}_{n}(t)

for
t>0
.
Note that for

t\geq\Big(\frac{V}{N}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{V}{N}+\tilde{M}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}},

we have

C_{B,d}\cdot\frac{V}{N}\cdot t\log\Big(2+\frac{\tilde{M}+V}{t}\Big)\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{n}t^{2}\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}\cdot\frac{V}{N}\cdot n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{N(\tilde{M}+V)n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{V^{\frac{1}{2}}(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big),

\displaystyle C_{B,d}\cdot\frac{V}{N}\cdot\tilde{M}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{\tilde{M}+V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{n}t^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}\Big(\frac{V}{N}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{N(\tilde{M}+V)n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{V^{\frac{1}{2}}(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big),

\displaystyle C_{B,d}\cdot\frac{V}{N}\cdot V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{n}t^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}\Big(\frac{V}{N}\Big)^{\frac{4}{5}}V^{\frac{1}{5}}n^{-\frac{2}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{NV^{\frac{1}{2}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}},

\displaystyle C_{B,d}\cdot\frac{V}{N}\cdot\tilde{M}V^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{-\frac{1}{4}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{\tilde{M}+V}{t}\Big)\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{s-1}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{n}t^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}\Big(\frac{V}{N}\Big)^{\frac{4}{9}}\tilde{M}^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}n^{-\frac{4}{9}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{N(\tilde{M}+V)n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{V^{\frac{1}{2}}(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4}{9}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{NV^{\frac{1}{2}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{9}},

and

\displaystyle C_{B,d}\cdot\frac{V}{N}\cdot\tilde{M}V^{\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\log\Big(2+\frac{V}{t}\Big)\Big]^{2(s-1)}\leq\frac{1}{5}\sqrt{n}t^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{\quad if \;}t\geq C_{B,d}\Big(\frac{V}{N}\Big)^{\frac{2}{5}}\tilde{M}^{\frac{2}{5}}V^{\frac{1}{5}}n^{-\frac{2}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{NV^{\frac{1}{2}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}.

Thus, if we let

\displaystyle\tilde{t}_{n}=\max\bigg\{C_{B,d}\cdot\Big(\frac{V}{N}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{V}{N}+\tilde{M}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log\Big(2+\frac{N(\tilde{M}+V)n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{V^{\frac{1}{2}}(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big),

\displaystyle\quad C_{B,d}\Big(\frac{V}{N}\Big)^{\frac{4}{9}}\tilde{M}^{\frac{4}{9}}V^{\frac{1}{9}}n^{-\frac{4}{9}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{N(\tilde{M}+V)n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{V^{\frac{1}{2}}(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4}{9}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{NV^{\frac{1}{2}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{9}}

\displaystyle\quad C_{B,d}\Big(\frac{V}{N}\Big)^{\frac{2}{5}}\Big(\frac{V}{N}+\tilde{M}\Big)^{\frac{2}{5}}V^{\frac{1}{5}}n^{-\frac{2}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{NV^{\frac{1}{2}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{(N\tilde{M}+V)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\bigg\},

then

\tilde{\Psi}_{n}(\tilde{t}_{n})\leq\sqrt{n}\tilde{t}_{n}^{2}.

This implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq r\tilde{t}_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq\tilde{\Psi}_{n}(r\tilde{t}_{n})\leq r\tilde{\Psi}_{n}(\tilde{t}_{n})\leq r\sqrt{n}\tilde{t}_{n}^{2}

for every
r\geq 1
.
Here the second inequality follows from the fact that
t\rightarrow\tilde{\Psi}_{n}(t)/t
is monotonically decreasing.

Now, let

\bar{t}_{n}=4\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}+t_{n}+\tilde{t}_{n}.

Then, it follows that

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq r\bar{t}_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq\Big(\frac{r\bar{t}_{n}}{t_{n}}\Big)\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}=r\sqrt{n}t_{n}\bar{t}_{n}\leq r\sqrt{n}\bar{t}_{n}^{2}

for every
r\geq 1
.
Similarly, we can show that for
r\geq 1
,

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq r\bar{t}_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}\bar{t}_{n}^{2}.

and

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\text{approx},\tilde{M}}(V)-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq r\bar{t}_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\leq r\sqrt{n}\bar{t}_{n}^{2}.

Therefore, by Theorem
E.12
and the fact that

\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2V}{N},

we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\tilde{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f_{0}\|_{p_{0},2}>t\big)\leq\epsilon

\displaystyle+C\cdot\frac{1+\tilde{M}^{3}}{t^{3}}\cdot\bar{t}_{n}^{3}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+(2V/N)^{3}+\tilde{M}^{3}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}t^{3}}

\displaystyle+C\cdot\frac{\tilde{M}^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{5}^{3}n^{\frac{3}{5}}+(2V/N)^{3}+\tilde{M}^{3})}{n^{3}t^{6}}

provided that
n
is sufficiently large and
t\geq\bar{t}_{n}
.
Using

\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}\leq C_{d}\Big(\frac{B}{N^{3}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}V,

we can derive from this result that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\tilde{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>t\big)\leq{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\|\tilde{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f_{0}\|_{p_{0},2}>t-C_{d}\Big(\frac{B}{N^{3}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}V\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq\epsilon+C\cdot\frac{1+(M+2V/N)^{3}}{(t-C_{d}(B/N^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}V)^{3}}\cdot\bar{t}_{n}^{3}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+(M+2V/N)^{3}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}(t-C_{d}(B/N^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}V)^{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+C\cdot\frac{(M+2V/N)^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{5}^{3}n^{\frac{3}{5}}+(M+2V/N)^{3})}{n^{3}(t-C_{d}(B/N^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}V)^{6}}

provided that
n
is sufficiently large and

t\geq C_{d}\Big(\frac{B}{N^{3}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}V+\bar{t}_{n}.

Because
N=\Omega(n^{4/15})
,

C_{d}\Big(\frac{B}{N^{3}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}V+\bar{t}_{n}\leq(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\cdot C_{B,d,V,M}\cdot n^{-\frac{2}{5}}+o(n^{-\frac{2}{5}}),

if
s=1
, and otherwise (
s\geq 2
),

\displaystyle C_{d}\Big(\frac{B}{N^{3}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}V

\displaystyle+\bar{t}_{n}\leq(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\cdot C_{B,d}(1+M^{\frac{1}{2}})^{\frac{4}{5}}V^{\frac{1}{5}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vn^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\cdot n^{-\frac{2}{5}}+O(n^{-\frac{2}{5}})

\displaystyle\leq(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\cdot C_{B,d,V,M}\bigg[\log\Big(2+\frac{Vn^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1+M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\cdot n^{-\frac{2}{5}}+O(n^{-\frac{2}{5}}),

where the constants underlying
o(\cdot)
and
O(\cdot)
depend on
B,d,V,M
, and the moments of
\xi_{i}
.
Hence, if

K\geq 2(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})\cdot C_{B,d,V,M},

then

Kn^{-\frac{2}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\geq C_{d}\Big(\frac{B}{N^{3}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}V+\bar{t}_{n}

for sufficiently large
n
.
For such
K
, again because
N=\Omega(n^{4/15})
, we have that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\tilde{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>Kn^{-\frac{2}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\epsilon+\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bigg[C\cdot\frac{1+(M+2V/N)^{3}}{(Kn^{-\frac{2}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}-C_{d}(B/N^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}V)^{3}}\cdot\bar{t}_{n}^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+(M+2V/N)^{3}}{n^{\frac{3}{2}}(Kn^{-\frac{2}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}-C_{d}(B/N^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}V)^{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\cdot\frac{(M+2V/N)^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{5}^{3}n^{\frac{3}{5}}+(M+2V/N)^{3})}{n^{3}(Kn^{-\frac{2}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}-C_{d}(B/N^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}V)^{6}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\epsilon+(1+4\|\xi_{1}\|_{5,1})^{3}\cdot C_{B,d,V,M}\cdot\frac{1}{K^{3}}+\|\xi_{1}\|_{5}^{3}\cdot CM^{3}\cdot\frac{1}{K^{6}}.

As a result, we can find
K>0
that satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\tilde{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}>Kn^{-\frac{2}{5}}(\log n)^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}\big)<2\epsilon

for sufficiently large
n
.
∎

E.3.7
Proof of Theorem
3.9

Here we almost follow the proof of
Fang, Guntuboyina and
Sen 2021
, Theorem 4.6, which itself obtains the ideas from
Blei, Gao and Li 2007
, Section 4.
As in
Fang, Guntuboyina and
Sen 2021
, we exploit Assouad’s lemma in the following form.

Lemma E.20
(
Van der Vaart 2000
, Lemma 24.3).

Let
q
be a positive integer.
Suppose for each
\eta\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
, we have
f_{\eta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}
with
V_{\text{mars}}(f_{\eta})\leq V
.
Then, the minimax risk
\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}
satisfies that

\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}\geq\frac{q}{8}\cdot\min_{\eta\neq\eta^{\prime}}\frac{\|f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}}{H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})}\cdot\min_{H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})=1}\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\big[K({\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta}},{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}})\big]}\bigg),

where
H
denotes the Hamming distance
H(\eta,\eta^{\prime}):=\sum_{j=1}^{q}1\{\eta_{j}\neq\eta^{\prime}_{j}\}
,
{\mathbb{P}}_{f}
is the probability distribution of
(y_{1},\dots,y_{n})
given
(X^{(1)},\dots,X^{(n)})
in (
20
) when
f^{*}=f
, and
K(\cdot,\cdot)
indicates the Kullback divergence between two probability distributions.

Remark E.21
.

What
Van der Vaart 2000
, Lemma 24.3 actually tells us in the setting of Lemma
E.20
is that

\max_{\eta}{\mathbb{E}}_{f_{\eta}}\|\hat{f}_{n}-f_{\eta}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\geq\frac{q}{8}\cdot\min_{\eta\neq\eta^{\prime}}\frac{\|f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}}{H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})}\cdot\min_{H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})=1}\big\|Q_{f_{\eta}}\wedge Q_{f_{\eta}^{\prime}}\big\|

for every estimator
\hat{f}_{n}
based on
(y_{1},X^{(1)}),\dots,(y_{n},X^{(n)})
, where
Q_{f}
is the joint distribution of
(y_{i},X^{(i)})
in (
20
) when
f^{*}=f
.
Here
\lVert Q_{f_{\eta}}\wedge Q_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}}\rVert
is defined by

\big\lVert Q_{f_{\eta}}\wedge Q_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}}\big\rVert=\int\min(q_{f_{\eta}},q_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}})\,d\mu,

where
\mu
is every measure for which both
q_{f_{\eta}}:=dQ_{f_{\eta}}/d\mu
and
q_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}}:=dQ_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}}/d\mu
exist.
However, we can derive Lemma
E.20
directly from
Van der Vaart 2000
, Lemma 24.3.
This is simply because we have

\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}=\inf_{\hat{f}_{n}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f^{*}\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}\\
V_{\text{mars}}(f^{*})\leq V\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}_{f^{*}}\|\hat{f}_{n}-f^{*}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\geq\inf_{\hat{f}_{n}}\max_{\eta}{\mathbb{E}}_{f_{\eta}}\|\hat{f}_{n}-f_{\eta}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\big\|Q_{f_{\eta}}\wedge Q_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}}\big\|

\displaystyle=\int\min(q_{f_{\eta}},q_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}})\,d\mu=1-\frac{1}{2}\int|q_{f_{\eta}}-q_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}}|\,d\mu

\displaystyle\geq 1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}K(Q_{f_{\eta}},Q_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}})}=1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\big[K({\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta}},{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}})\big]}.

Here the inequality is due to the Pinsker–Csiszár–Kullback inequality (see, e.g.,
Wainwright 2019
, Lemma 15.2).

Let
l
be a positive integer whose value will be specified momentarily.
Consider the set

P_{l}=\bigg\{(p_{1},\dots,p_{s})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{s}:\sum_{j=1}^{s}p_{j}=l\bigg\}

and for each
p\in P_{l}
, let

I_{p}=\big\{(i_{1},\dots,i_{s}):i_{j}\in[2^{p_{j}}]\mbox{ for each }j\in[s]\big\}.

Clearly,
|I_{p}|=2^{l}
for every
p\in P_{l}
.
Also, we have

|P_{l}|=\binom{s+l-1}{s-1}\geq\frac{l^{s-1}}{(s-1)!}:=a_{s}l^{s-1}.

Now, we choose
l
as

l=\bigg\lceil\frac{1}{5\log 2}\Big\{\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)-(s-1)\log\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\Big\}\bigg\rceil,

where
C_{B,s}=B2^{-12s+1}(10\log 2)^{s-1}/a_{s}
.
Here
\lceil x\rceil
indicates the least integer greater than or equal to
x
.
The reason why we choose these particular
C_{B,s}
and
l
will become clear at the later stage of the proof.
Note that

2^{-l}\leq\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{s-1}{5}}<2^{-l+1}.

(71)

Let

Q=\big\{(p,i):p\in P_{l}\mbox{ and }i\in I_{p}\big\}

and let
q=|Q|=|P_{l}|\cdot 2^{l}
.
Throughout this proof, we will index the components of every
\eta\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
with the set
Q
.
For a positive integer
m
and
k\in[2^{m}]
, we let
\phi_{m,k}
be the real-valued function on
(0,1)
defined by

\displaystyle\phi_{m,k}(x)=\begin{cases}1&\mbox{if }x\in\big((k-1)2^{-m},(k-\frac{7}{8})2^{-m}\big)\cup\big((k-\frac{5}{8})2^{-m},(k-\frac{1}{2})2^{-m}\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad\cup\big((k-\frac{3}{8})2^{-m},(k-\frac{1}{8})2^{-m}\big)\\
-1&\mbox{if }x\in\big((k-\frac{7}{8})2^{-m},(k-\frac{5}{8})2^{-m}\big)\cup\big((k-\frac{1}{2})2^{-m},(k-\frac{3}{8})2^{-m}\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad\cup\big((k-\frac{1}{8})2^{-m},k2^{-m}\big)\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We now construct our function
f_{\eta}
for each
\eta\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
based on these functions
\phi_{m,k}
.
For each
\eta\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
, we let
\nu_{\eta}
be the signed measure on
(0,1)^{s}
defined by

d\nu_{\eta}(t)=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\eta_{p,i}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{s}\phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\bigg)dt,

and let
f_{\eta}
denote the real-valued function on
[0,1]^{d}
defined by

f_{\eta}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\int_{(0,1)^{s}}\prod_{j=1}^{s}(x_{j}-t_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\eta}(t).

The following lemma, whose proof is given in Appendix
E.7.8
, then presents the key properties of the functions
f_{\eta}
.

Lemma E.22
.

For each
\eta\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
,
f_{\eta}
satisfies

V_{\text{mars}}(f_{\eta})\leq V.

(72)

Also, we have

\max_{H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})=1}\|f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\leq\frac{BV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-5l-12s+2}.

(73)

and

\min_{\eta\neq\eta^{\prime}}\frac{\|f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}}{H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})}\geq\frac{bV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-5l-14s+2}.

(74)

Since the Kullback divergence between
{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}}
for
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
can be computed by

K({\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta}},{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}})=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{\eta}(X^{(i)})-f_{\eta^{\prime}}(X^{(i)})\big)^{2},

we can obtain from (
73
) that

\displaystyle\max_{H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})=1}{\mathbb{E}}\big[K({\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta}},{\mathbb{P}}_{f_{\eta^{\prime}}})\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2\sigma^{2}}\cdot\max_{H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})=1}\|f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\leq\frac{BnV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-5l-12s+1}.

(75)

We are now ready to apply Lemma
E.20
.
By Lemma
E.20
together with (
74
) and (
75
), the minimax risk
\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}
satisfies that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{q}{8}\cdot\frac{bV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-5l-14s+2}\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{BnV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-5l-12s}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=bV^{2}2^{-4l-14s-1}\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2(10\log 2)^{s-1}}\cdot\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\cdot\frac{2^{-5l}}{l^{s-1}}}\bigg).

Recall that we let
C_{B,s}=B2^{-12s+1}(10\log 2)^{s-1}/a_{s}
.
Our choice of
l
then implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2(10\log 2)^{s-1}}\cdot\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\cdot\frac{2^{-5l}}{l^{s-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Bigg[\frac{\log\big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)}{2\Big(\log\big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)-(s-1)\log\log\big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)\Big)}\Bigg]^{s-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\bigg[2\bigg(1-(s-1)\frac{\log\log\big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)}{\log\big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\big)}\bigg)\bigg]^{-(s-1)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\bigg[2\bigg(1-(s-1)\Big\{\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\Big\}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)\bigg]^{-(s-1)}.

Here we use

l\geq\frac{1}{5\log 2}\bigg\{\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)-(s-1)\log\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg\}

and (
71
) for the first inequality, and the last one is due to
\log\log x/\log x\leq(\log x)^{-1/2}
, which holds for all
x>1
.
Thus, if

n\geq\frac{e^{4s^{2}}}{C_{B,s}}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{V^{2}},

we have

\frac{1}{2(10\log 2)^{s-1}}\cdot\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\cdot\frac{2^{-5l}}{l^{s-1}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Big(1+\frac{1}{s}\Big)^{-(s-1)}\leq\frac{1}{2},

and therefore,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)\cdot bV^{2}2^{-4l-14s-1}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)\cdot bV^{2}2^{-14s-5}\cdot\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{4}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}}

\displaystyle\geq C_{b,B,s}\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}V^{\frac{1}{2}}}{n}\Big)^{\frac{4}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}},

where
C_{b,B,s}=(1-1/\sqrt{2})\cdot b2^{-14s-5}C_{B,s}^{-4/5}
.
Here for the second inequality, we use (
71
) again.
Now, note that

\log\Big(\frac{C_{B,s}nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\geq\frac{1}{2}\log\Big(\frac{nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)

if
n\geq(1/C_{B,s}^{2})\cdot(\sigma^{2}/V^{2})
.
Consequently, by further assuming that

n\geq\max\Big\{\frac{e^{4s^{2}}}{C_{B,s}}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{V^{2}},\frac{1}{C_{B,s}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{V^{2}}\Big\},

we can derive the conclusion

\mathfrak{M}^{d,s}_{n,V}\geq C_{b,B,s}^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\sigma^{2}V^{\frac{1}{2}}}{n}\Big)^{\frac{4}{5}}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{nV^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{4(s-1)}{5}},

where
C_{b,B,s}^{\prime}=C_{b,B,s}\cdot 2^{-4(s-1)/5}
.
∎

E.4
Proofs of Lemmas and Propositions in Section
4

E.4.1
Proof of Proposition
4.1

Suppose
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}
.
Then, there exist
a_{\mathbf{0}}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and a collection of finite signed measures
\{\nu_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\mbox{ and }|\alpha|\leq s\}
as in (
3
) such that
f=f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}
.
For each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
, let
g_{\alpha}
be the function on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
defined by

g_{\alpha}(x^{(\alpha)})=\nu_{\alpha}([\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]\cap[0,1)^{|\alpha|})

for
x^{(\alpha)}\in[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
.
Clearly,
g_{\alpha}
is coordinate-wise right-continuous and coordinate-wise left-continuous at each point
x^{(\alpha)}=(x_{j},j\in S(\alpha))\in[0,1]^{|\alpha|}\setminus[0,1)^{|\alpha|}
with respect to all the
j^{\text{th}}
coordinates where
x_{j}=1
.
Also, by Proposition
A.3
, we can see that
g_{\alpha}
has finite HK0 variation.
Moreover, we have

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-t_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{\prod\limits_{j\in S(\alpha)}[0,x_{j})}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-t_{j})\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{\prod\limits_{j\in S(\alpha)}[0,x_{j})}\int_{\prod\limits_{j\in S(\alpha)}[t_{j},x_{j})}1\,ds^{(\alpha)}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{\prod\limits_{j\in S(\alpha)}[0,x_{j})}\int_{\prod\limits_{j\in S(\alpha)}[0,s_{j}]}1\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\,ds^{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{\prod\limits_{j\in S(\alpha)}[0,x_{j})}\nu_{\alpha}([\mathbf{0},s^{(\alpha)}])\,ds^{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)})\,ds^{(\alpha)}

for all
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}
.
Thus,
f
can be represented as in (
31
) with
a_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and the collection of functions
\{g_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\mbox{ and }|\alpha|\leq s\}
that has the desired properties.

Conversely, suppose a real-valued function
f
on
[0,1]^{d}
is of the form

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

for some
a_{\mathbf{0}}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and for some collection of functions
\{g_{\alpha}:\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\mbox{ and }|\alpha|\leq s\}
, where for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
,
g_{\alpha}:[0,1]^{|\alpha|}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
has finite HK0 variation, is coordinate-wise right-continuous, and is coordinate-wise left-continuous at each point
x^{(\alpha)}=(x_{j},j\in S(\alpha))\in[0,1]^{|\alpha|}\setminus[0,1)^{|\alpha|}
with respect to all the
j^{\text{th}}
coordinates where
x_{j}=1
.
For each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
, by Proposition
A.3
again, there exists a finite signed measure
\nu_{\alpha}
on
[0,1)^{|\alpha|}
such that

g_{\alpha}(x^{(\alpha)})=\nu_{\alpha}([\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]\cap[0,1)^{|\alpha|})\qquad\text{for $x^{(\alpha)}\in[0,1]^{|\alpha|}$}

and

|\nu_{\alpha}|([0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\})=V_{\text{HK0}}(g_{\alpha}).

Hence, the function
f
can be written as

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{\prod\limits_{j\in S(\alpha)}[0,x_{j})}\nu_{\alpha}([\mathbf{0},t^{(\alpha)}])\,dt^{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)}),

which means that
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}
.
Moreover, we can represent the complexity measure of
f
as

V_{\text{mars}}(f)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}|\nu_{\alpha}|([0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}V_{\text{HK0}}(g_{\alpha}).

∎

E.4.2
Proof of Proposition
4.2

Here we prove a stronger statement that directly implies Proposition
4.2
.
Here we show that for
f
in the condition (
31
), the derivatives of the interval functions associated with
f
exist and equal to
g_{\alpha}
in the same condition almost everywhere (with respect to the Lebesgue measure).
We start by introducing interval functions, their derivatives, and some related concepts.
One can refer to
Łojasiewicz 1988
for more detailed introduction of them.

Two closed real intervals are said to be adjoining if they have a common end point and do not share interior points.
For example,
[0,1]
and
[1,2]
are two adjoining closed intervals.
Similarly, we say that two
m
-dimensional axis-aligned closed rectangles

[x,y]=\prod_{i=1}^{m}[x_{i},y_{i}]\ \mbox{ and }\ [z,w]=\prod_{i=1}^{m}[z_{i},w_{i}]

are adjoining if
[x_{k},y_{k}]
and
[z_{k},w_{k}]
are adjoining for some
k\in[m]
and
[x_{i},y_{i}]=[z_{i},w_{i}]
for all
i\neq k
.
For instance,
[0,1]\times[0,1]
and
[0,1]\times[1,2]
are two adjoining two-dimensional axis-aligned closed rectangles.
Note that for two adjoining axis-aligned closed rectangles, their union is also an axis-aligned closed rectangle.
A real-valued function
F
defined on a class of
m
-dimensional axis-aligned closed rectangles is then called an additive interval function if

F(R\cup R^{\prime})=F(R)+F(R^{\prime})

for every pair of
m
-dimensional adjoining axis-aligned closed rectangles
R
and
R^{\prime}
.

For an axis-aligned closed rectangle

[x,y]=\prod_{i=1}^{m}[x_{i},y_{i}],

|[x,y]|
denotes the volume of the rectangle
|[x,y]|=(y_{1}-x_{1})\cdots(y_{m}-x_{m})
.
Also,
[x,y]
is called a cube if it is the product of closed intervals of equal length, i.e.,
y_{1}-x_{1}=\cdots=y_{m}-x_{m}
.
The upper and the lower derivative of an additive interval function
F
at
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
are then defined by

\overline{D}F(x)=\limsup_{x\in C,|C|\rightarrow 0}\frac{F(C)}{|C|}\ \text{ and }\ \underline{D}F(x)=\liminf_{x\in C,|C|\rightarrow 0}\frac{F(C)}{|C|}

where
\limsup
and
\liminf
are taken over
m
-dimensional cubes
C
containing
x
.
If the two derivatives coincide, we say the derivative of
F
at
x
exists and define it as the common value

DF(x)=\lim_{x\in C,|C|\rightarrow 0}\frac{F(C)}{|C|}.

The Lebesgue differentiation theorem (see, e.g.,
Rudin 1987
, Theorem
7.10) can be stated in terms of interval functions and their derivatives as follows.
The following theorem plays a key role in identifying
g_{\alpha}
in the condition (
31
).

Theorem E.23
(
Łojasiewicz 1988
, Theorem 7.1.7).

Let
f
be an integrable real-valued function defined on an axis-aligned closed rectangle
R_{0}
.
Suppose that
F
is the additive interval function defined by

F(R)=\int_{R}f(x)dx

for axis-aligned closed rectangles
R\subseteq R_{0}
.
Then, it follows that
DF=f
almost everywhere (with respect to the Lebesgue measure) on
R_{0}
.

We now define for a real-valued function
f
on
[0,1]^{d}
the interval functions associated with
f
.
For each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
, denote by
\Delta_{\alpha}f
the additive interval function which is defined on the class of axis-aligned closed rectangles contained in
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
and which is defined by

\Delta_{\alpha}f([x^{(\alpha)},y^{(\alpha)}])=\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}{\{0,1\}}}(-1)^{\sum\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\delta_{k}}f\big(\widetilde{xy}_{\delta}^{(\alpha)}\big)

where
x^{(\alpha)}=(x_{k},k\in S(\alpha))
,
y^{(\alpha)}=(y_{k},k\in S(\alpha))
, and
\widetilde{xy}_{\delta}^{(\alpha)}
is the
d
-dimensional vector with

\big(\widetilde{xy}_{\delta}^{(\alpha)}\big)_{k}=\begin{cases}\delta_{k}x_{k}+(1-\delta_{k})y_{k}&\mbox{if }k\in S(\alpha)\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

for
k\in[d]
.
For example, if
d=3
and
\alpha=(1,0,1)
,
\Delta_{1,0,1}f
is defined by

\Delta_{1,0,1}f([x_{1},y_{1}]\times[x_{3},y_{3}])=f(y_{1},0,y_{3})-f(y_{1},0,x_{3})-f(x_{1},0,y_{3})+f(x_{1},0,x_{3}).

Also, if
\alpha=\mathbf{1}=(1,\dots,1)
,
\Delta_{\mathbf{1}}f([x,y])
coincides with the quasi-volume of
f
on
[x,y]
for all
x,y\in[0,1]^{d}
.
We call these
\Delta_{\alpha}f
the interval functions associated with
f
.

We are ready to state and prove the stronger statement describing the relationship between
f
and
g_{\alpha}
in the condition (
31
).
It is clear from the definitions above that Proposition
4.2
directly follows from this result.

Proposition E.24
.

Suppose that the condition (
31
) holds.
Then, for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
,
D\Delta_{\alpha}f=0
if
|\alpha|>s
, and
D\Delta_{\alpha}f=g_{\alpha}
almost everywhere (with respect to the Lebesgue measure) on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
if
|\alpha|\leq s
.

Recall that the condition (
31
) assumes that

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

for
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}
, where
a_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
g_{\alpha}
is a real-valued function on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
.
By letting
g_{\alpha}=0
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|>s
, we can rewrite it as

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

for
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}
.
Then, for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{\alpha}f([x^{(\alpha)},y^{(\alpha)}])=\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}{\{0,1\}}}(-1)^{\sum\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\delta_{k}}f\big(\widetilde{xy}_{\delta}^{(\alpha)}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}{\{0,1\}}}(-1)^{\sum\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\delta_{k}}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\int_{\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha^{\prime})}[0,\delta_{k}x_{k}+(1-\delta_{k})y_{k}]}g_{\alpha^{\prime}}(t^{(\alpha^{\prime})})\,dt^{(\alpha^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
\alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha\end{subarray}}\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}{\{0,1\}}}(-1)^{\sum\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\delta_{k}}\cdot\int_{\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha^{\prime})}[0,\delta_{k}x_{k}+(1-\delta_{k})y_{k}]}g_{\alpha^{\prime}}(t^{(\alpha^{\prime})})\,dt^{(\alpha^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}{\{0,1\}}}(-1)^{\sum\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\delta_{k}}\cdot\int_{\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0,\delta_{k}x_{k}+(1-\delta_{k})y_{k}]}g_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle\qquad\ =\int_{\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}[x_{k},y_{k}]}g_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}=\int_{[x^{(\alpha)},y^{(\alpha)}]}g_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

for
x^{(\alpha)},y^{(\alpha)}\in[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
.
Thus, by Theorem
E.23
, the derivative
D\Delta_{\alpha}f
exists and equals to
g_{\alpha}
almost everywhere (with respect to the Lebesgue measure) for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
.
Because we let
g_{\alpha}=0
for
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|>s
, this means that
D\Delta_{\alpha}f=0
if
|\alpha|>s
, and
D\Delta_{\alpha}f=g_{\alpha}
almost everywhere if
|\alpha|\leq s
.
∎

E.4.3
Proof of Lemma
4.3

We use the following lemma for proving Lemma
4.3
.
This lemma will be used again for proving Lemma
A.4
.
We provide our proof of Lemma
E.25
right after the proof of Lemma
4.3
.

Lemma E.25
.

If a real-valued function
g
defined on
[0,1]^{m}
is smooth in the sense that
g^{(\alpha)}
exists and is continuous on
[0,1]^{m}
for every
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}
, then
g
satisfies

g(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=g(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

for all
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\in[0,1]^{m}
.
Here
\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)}
is an
m
-dimensional extension of
t^{(\alpha)}
where

\displaystyle\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)}_{k}=\begin{cases}t_{k}&\mbox{if }k\in S(\alpha)\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Suppose
f
is smooth in the sense that it satisfies the two conditions in the lemma and assume that
f^{(\alpha)}=0
for every
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|>s
.
For each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
, let
g_{\alpha}
be the function on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
defined by

g_{\alpha}(x^{(\alpha)})=f^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{x}^{(\alpha)})

for
x^{(\alpha)}\in[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
.
Note that since
f^{(\alpha)}
is continuous on
\bar{T}^{(\alpha)}
,
g_{\alpha}
is continuous on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
.
Also, since
f^{(\beta)}
exists and is continuous on
\bar{T}^{(\alpha)}
for every
\beta\in J_{\alpha}
, we can see that
g_{\alpha}^{(\delta)}
exists and is continuous on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
for every
\delta\in\{0,1\}^{|\alpha|}
.
Thus, by Lemma
A.4
, it follows that
g_{\alpha}
has finite HK0 variation and

V_{\text{HK0}}(g_{\alpha})=\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{\bar{T}_{\alpha}^{(\delta)}}|g_{\alpha}^{(\delta)}|,

where

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{\alpha}^{(\delta)}:=\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\bar{T}^{(\delta)}_{k}~\text{ where }~\bar{T}^{(\delta)}_{k}=\begin{cases}(0,1)&\mbox{if }\delta_{k}=\max_{j}\delta_{j}\\
\{0\}&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Moreover, by the assumption that
f^{(\alpha)}
exists and is continuous on
[0,1]^{d}
for every
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, Lemma
E.25
implies that

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})

\displaystyle=f(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}f^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle=f(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

for all
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}
.
For these reasons, by Proposition
4.1
, we can conclude that
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}
and that

\displaystyle V_{\text{mars}}(f)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}V_{\text{HK0}}(g_{\alpha})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{\bar{T}_{\alpha}^{(\delta)}}|g_{\alpha}^{(\delta)}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{\beta\in J_{\alpha}}\int_{\bar{T}^{(\beta)}}|f^{(\beta)}|.

If
s=d
,
V_{\text{mars}}(f)
can also be simplified as

V_{\text{mars}}(f)=\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\sum_{\beta\in J_{\alpha}}\int_{\bar{T}^{(\beta)}}|f^{(\beta)}|=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\{0,1,2\}^{d}\\
\max_{j}\beta_{j}=2\end{subarray}}\int_{\bar{T}^{(\beta)}}|f^{(\beta)}|.

∎

Note that for
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
,

\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}=\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\{0,1\}}(-1)^{\sum\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\delta_{k}}\cdot g\big(\tilde{x}_{\delta}^{(\alpha)}\big),

where
\tilde{x}_{\delta}^{(\alpha)}
is the
m
-dimensional vector with

\big(\tilde{x}_{\delta}^{(\alpha)}\big)_{k}=\begin{cases}(1-\delta_{k})x_{k}&\mbox{if }k\in S(\alpha)\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

for
k\in[m]
.
It can be easily proved by the fundamental theorem of calculus, and we use the smoothness assumption on
g
here.
For example, if
m=2
and
\alpha=(1,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{[0,x_{1}]\times[0,x_{2}]}g^{(1,1)}(t_{1},t_{2})\,d(t_{1},t_{2})=\int_{0}^{x_{1}}\int_{0}^{x_{2}}g^{(1,1)}(t_{1},t_{2})\,dt_{2}\,dt_{1}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{0}^{x_{1}}\big(g^{(1,0)}(t_{1},x_{2})-g^{(1,0)}(t_{1},0)\big)\,dt_{1}=\int_{0}^{x_{1}}g^{(1,0)}(t_{1},x_{2})\,dt_{1}-\int_{0}^{x_{1}}g^{(1,0)}(t_{1},0)\,dt_{1}

\displaystyle\quad=g(x_{1},x_{2})-g(0,x_{2})-g(x_{1},0)+g(0,0)=\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{2}}(-1)^{\delta_{1}+\delta_{2}}\cdot g\big(\tilde{x}_{\delta}^{(1,1)}\big),

and we use that
g,g^{(1,0)},g^{(0,1)}
, and
g^{(1,1)}
are continuous on
[0,1]^{2}
for this argument.
Thus,

\displaystyle g(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle\quad=g(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\{0,1\}}(-1)^{\sum\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\delta_{k}}\cdot g\big(\tilde{x}_{\delta}^{(\alpha)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\{0,1\}}(-1)^{\sum\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\delta_{k}}\cdot g\big(\tilde{x}_{\delta}^{(\alpha)}\big)=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{m}}g(\tilde{x}^{(\alpha^{\prime})})\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\\
\alpha\geq\alpha^{\prime}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|\alpha|-|\alpha^{\prime}|}.

Here the last equality results from counting the number of
g(\tilde{x}^{(\alpha^{\prime})})
for each
\alpha^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{m}
.
Note that if
\alpha^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{1}
,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\\
\alpha\geq\alpha^{\prime}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|\alpha|-|\alpha^{\prime}|}=\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S_{0}(\alpha^{\prime})}\{0,1\}}(-1)^{\sum\limits_{k\in S_{0}(\alpha^{\prime})}\delta_{k}}=(1-1)^{|S_{0}(\alpha^{\prime})|}=0,

where
S_{0}(\alpha^{\prime})=\{j\in[m]:\alpha^{\prime}_{j}=0\}
, and that if
\alpha^{\prime}=\mathbf{1}
,

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\\
\alpha\geq\alpha^{\prime}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|\alpha|-|\alpha^{\prime}|}=1.

As a result, it follows that

g(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}=g(\tilde{x}^{(\mathbf{1})})=g(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})

as desired.
∎

E.5
Proofs of Lemmas and Propositions in Appendix
A

E.5.1
Proof of Proposition
A.3

Our proof of Proposition
A.3
is based on
Aistleitner and
Dick 2015
, Theorem 3, which we restate as Theorem
E.26
below.

Aistleitner and
Dick 2015
, Theorem 3 connects functions on
[0,1]^{m}
that has finite HK0 variation and is coordinate-wise right-continuous to the cumulative distribution functions of finite signed measures on
[0,1]^{m}
.
We will use this theorem again for proving Lemma
A.4
.

Theorem E.26
(
Aistleitner and
Dick 2015
, Theorem 3).

Suppose a real-valued function
g
defined on
[0,1]^{m}
has finite HK0 variation and is coordinate-wise right-continuous.
Then, there exists a unique finite signed measure
\nu
on
[0,1]^{m}
such that

g(x)=\nu([\mathbf{0},x])\qquad\text{for every $x\in[0,1]^{m}$}.

(76)

Also, if a real-valued function
g
defined on
[0,1]^{m}
is of the form (
76
) for some finite signed measure
\nu
on
[0,1]^{m}
, then
g
has finite HK0 variation and

|\nu|([0,1]^{m})=V_{\text{HK0}}(g)+|g(\mathbf{0})|.

Assume that a real-valued function
g
defined on
[0,1]^{m}
has finite HK0 variation, is coordinate-wise right-continuous, and is coordinate-wise left-continuous at each point
x\in[0,1]^{m}\setminus[0,1)^{m}
with respect to all the
j^{\text{th}}
coordinates where
x_{j}=1
.
Since
g
has finite HK0 variation and is coordinate-wise right-continuous, by Theorem
E.26
, there exists a finite signed measure
\bar{\nu}
on
[0,1]^{m}
such that

g(x)=\bar{\nu}([\mathbf{0},x])

for all
x\in[0,1]^{m}
.
Let
\mu
be the finite signed measure on
[0,1]^{m}
for which

\mu(E)=\bar{\nu}(E\cap[0,1)^{m}).

Then, it follows that

g(x)=\mu([\mathbf{0},x])

for all
x\in[0,1]^{m}
.
Indeed, if
x\in[0,1)^{m}
, then

g(x)=\bar{\nu}([\mathbf{0},x])=\bar{\nu}([\mathbf{0},x]\cap[0,1)^{m})=\mu([\mathbf{0},x]).

Otherwise, without loss of generality, let
x=(1,\dots,1,x_{k+1},\dots,x_{m})
where
x_{k+1},\dots,x_{m}

<1
for some
k\in[m]
.
Then, by the left-continuity of
g
,

\displaystyle g(x)

\displaystyle=\lim_{y_{1}\uparrow 1}\cdots\lim_{y_{k}\uparrow 1}g(y_{1},\dots,y_{k},x_{k+1},\dots,x_{m})

\displaystyle=\lim_{y_{1}\uparrow 1}\cdots\lim_{y_{k}\uparrow 1}\bar{\nu}([\mathbf{0},(y_{1},\dots,y_{k},x_{k+1},\dots,x_{m})])

\displaystyle=\bar{\nu}([0,1)^{k}\times[0,x_{k+1}]\times\dots\times[0,x_{m}])

\displaystyle=\bar{\nu}([\mathbf{0},x]\cap[0,1)^{m})=\mu([\mathbf{0},x]),

as well.
We thus have the two signed measure
\bar{\nu}
and
\mu
satisfying (
76
), and the uniqueness in Theorem
E.26
implies that
\bar{\nu}=\mu
, i.e.,

\bar{\nu}(E)=\mu(E)=\bar{\nu}(E\cap[0,1)^{m}).

Therefore, if we let
\nu
be the finite signed measure on
[0,1)^{m}
for which

\nu(E)=\bar{\nu}(E),

then for every
x\in[0,1]^{m}
,

g(x)=\bar{\nu}([\mathbf{0},x])=\bar{\nu}([\mathbf{0},x]\cap[0,1)^{m})=\nu([\mathbf{0},x]\cap[0,1)^{m})

as desired.
The uniqueness of such signed measure directly follows from the uniqueness of the signed measure in Theorem
E.26
.

Next, suppose a real-valued function
g
on
[0,1]^{m}
is of the form (
38
) for some signed measure
\nu
on
[0,1)^{m}
.
Let
\bar{\nu}
be the finite signed measure on
[0,1]^{m}
for which

\bar{\nu}(E)=\nu(E\cap[0,1)^{m}).

Then, clearly,

g(x)=\nu([\mathbf{0},x]\cap[0,1)^{m})=\bar{\nu}([\mathbf{0},x])

for every
x\in[0,1]^{m}
.
By Theorem
E.26
,
g
thus has finite HK0 variation and

|\bar{\nu}|([0,1]^{m})=V_{\text{HK0}}(g)+|g(\mathbf{0})|.

Also, since
|g(\mathbf{0})|=|\nu(\{0\})|=|\nu|(\{0\})
and
|\bar{\nu}|([0,1]^{m})=|\nu|([0,1)^{m})
, it follows that

V_{\text{HK0}}(g)=|\nu|([0,1)^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}).

∎

E.5.2
Proof of Lemma
A.4

First, note that since
g
is smooth in the sense of Lemma
E.25
, we have

\begin{split}g(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})&=g(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{[\mathbf{0},x^{(\alpha)}]}g^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}\end{split}

(77)

for all
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\in[0,1]^{m}
.
For each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
, let
\mu_{\alpha}
be the signed measure on
(0,1]^{|\alpha|}
defined by

d\mu_{\alpha}=g^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}.

Also, for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
, consider the projection
\pi_{\alpha}:{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{|\alpha|}
for which

\pi_{\alpha}\big((t_{k},k\in[m])\big)=(t_{k},k\in S(\alpha))

and let
R^{(\alpha)}=\prod_{k=1}^{m}R^{(\alpha)}_{k}
where

\displaystyle R^{(\alpha)}_{k}=\begin{cases}(0,1]&\mbox{if }\alpha_{k}=\max_{j}\alpha_{j}\\
\{0\}&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Next, we patch together these signed measures and form the signed measure
\mu
on
[0,1]^{m}
by

\mu(E)=g(\mathbf{0})\cdot 1\{\mathbf{0}\in E\}+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\mu_{\alpha}(\pi_{\alpha}(E\cap R^{(\alpha)})).

Then, we can observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\mu|([0,1]^{m})&=|g(\mathbf{0})|+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}|\mu_{\alpha}|((0,1]^{|\alpha|})\\
&=|g(\mathbf{0})|+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{(0,1]^{|\alpha|}}|g^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)})|\,dt^{(\alpha)}\\
&=|g(\mathbf{0})|+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{\bar{T}^{(\alpha)}}|g^{(\alpha)}|.\end{split}

(78)

By (
77
), we also have

\displaystyle g(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=g(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}(0,x_{k}]}g^{(\alpha)}(\tilde{t}^{(\alpha)})\,dt^{(\alpha)}

\displaystyle\qquad=g(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}(0,x_{k}]}\,d\mu_{\alpha}=g(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\mu_{\alpha}\Big(\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}(0,x_{k}]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad=g(\mathbf{0})\cdot 1\{\mathbf{0}\in[\mathbf{0},x]\}+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\mu_{\alpha}\Big(\pi_{\alpha}\big([\mathbf{0},x]\cap R^{(\alpha)}\big)\Big)=\mu([\mathbf{0},x])

for all
x=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{m})\in[0,1]^{m}
.
Therefore, Theorem
E.26
implies that
g
has finite HK0 variation and

|\mu|([0,1]^{m})=V_{\text{HK0}}(g)+|g(\mathbf{0})|.

Combining this with the equation (
78
), we can derive that

V_{\text{HK0}}(g)=\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\int_{\bar{T}^{(\alpha)}}|g^{(\alpha)}|.

∎

E.6
Proof of Proposition
C.3

Before proving Proposition
C.3
, we extend the definition of the operator
H^{(2)}
to general orders.
This helps understand connection between
\theta
and
H^{(2)}\theta
for vectors
\theta
indexed with the set
I_{0}=[0:(n_{1}-1)]\times\dots\times[0:(n_{d}-1)]
.

Suppose that
\theta
is an
n
-dimensional vector indexed with the set
I_{0}
.
We let
H^{(0)}\theta=\theta
and for each positive integer
r
, let
H^{(r)}\theta
be the
n
-dimensional vector indexed by
i\in I_{0}
, where

\displaystyle(H^{(r)}\theta)_{i}=\begin{cases}(D^{(\alpha)}\theta)_{i}&\mbox{if }i\in I_{0}^{(\alpha)}\mbox{ for some }\alpha\in[0:r]^{d}\mbox{ with }\max_{j}\alpha_{j}=r\\
(D^{(i)}\theta)_{i}&\mbox{if }i\in[0:(r-1)]^{d}.\end{cases}

Recall that

\biguplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in[0:r]^{d}\\
\max_{j}\alpha_{j}=r\end{subarray}}I_{0}^{(\alpha)}=I_{0}\setminus[0:(r-1)]^{d}

and note that the definition agrees with the former one when
r=2
.
Next, let
A^{(0)}=L_{I_{0}}
and for each positive integer
r
, let
A^{(r)}
be the block matrix that is indexed with the set
I_{0}
and consists of the identity block matrix indexed with
[0:(r-1)]^{d}
and all the blocks
L_{I_{0}^{(\alpha)}}
for
\alpha\in[0:r]^{d}
with
\max_{j}\alpha_{j}=r
.
For an index set
I
, we use the notation
L_{I}
to indicate the
|I|\times|I|
matrix whose rows and columns are indexed by
i,j\in I
, where

(L_{I})_{ij}=\mathbf{1}\{i\geq j\}:=\prod_{k}\mathbf{1}\{i_{k}\geq j_{k}\}

for
i,j\in I
.
Then, one can verify that for each
r\geq 0
,

(H^{(r)}\theta)_{i}=\sum_{j\in I_{0}^{(\alpha)}}\mathbf{1}\{i\geq j\}(H^{(r+1)}\theta)_{j}

for every
i\in I_{0}^{(\alpha)}
for
\alpha\in[0:r]^{d}
with
\max_{j}\alpha_{j}=r
.
We thus have

H^{(r)}\theta=A^{(r)}(H^{(r+1)}\theta)

for all
r\geq 0
, and this leads to
\theta=A^{(0)}A^{(1)}\cdots A^{(r-1)}H^{(r)}\theta
for all
r\geq 0
.
Especially, when
r=2
, we have

\theta=A^{(0)}A^{(1)}(H^{(2)}\theta),

(79)

which can be explicitly written as

\theta_{i}=(H^{(2)}\theta)_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\sum_{l\in I_{0}^{(\alpha)}}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}\binom{i_{k}-l_{k}+\alpha_{k}}{\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i\geq l\}\cdot(H^{(2)}\theta)_{l}\bigg]

(80)

for
i\in I_{0}
.
Moreover, here we additionally note that
V_{2}(\theta)
can be represented in
H^{(2)}\theta
as

\displaystyle V_{2}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\{0,1,2\}^{d}\\
\max_{j}\beta_{j}=2\end{subarray}}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\beta_{k}-\mathbf{1}\{\beta_{k}=2\}}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{i\in I_{0}^{(\beta)}}\big|(D^{(\beta)}\theta)_{i}\big|\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\sum_{\beta\in J_{\alpha}}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\beta_{k}-\mathbf{1}\{\beta_{k}=2\}}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{i\in I_{0}^{(\beta)}}\big|(D^{(\beta)}\theta)_{i}\big|\bigg]

(81)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{\beta\in J_{\alpha}}\sum_{i\in I_{0}^{(\beta)}}\big|(D^{(\beta)}\theta)_{i}\big|\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{i\in I_{0}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\alpha\}}\big|(H^{(2)}\theta)_{i}\big|\bigg].

We first re-index the rows of
M
(defined in Proposition
2.2
) with the set
I_{0}=[0:(n_{1}-1)]\times\dots\times[0:(n_{d}-1)]
, i.e., we let

M_{i,(\alpha,l)}=\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\Big(\frac{i_{k}}{n_{k}}-\frac{l_{k}}{n_{k}}\Big)_{+}

for
i\in I_{0}
and
(\alpha,l)\in J
.
We can then observe that

M_{i,(\alpha,l)}=0

if
l_{k}=n_{k}-1
for some
k\in[d]
.
This suggests that we can remove such all-zero columns from
M
and the corresponding components from
\gamma
in the problem (
13
).
Consider the submatrix
\tilde{M}
of
M
consisting of the components that have column indices in
\tilde{J}
.
Then, from the observation above, it can be easily checked that for a solution
(\hat{a}_{\mathbf{0}},\hat{\omega}^{d}_{n,V})\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{|\tilde{J}|}
to the new lasso problem

(\hat{a}_{\mathbf{0}},\hat{\omega}^{d}_{n,V})\in\underset{a_{\mathbf{0}}\in{\mathbb{R}},\omega\in{\mathbb{R}}^{|\tilde{J}|}}{\mathop{\rm argmin}}\Bigg\{\big\lVert y-a_{\mathbf{0}}\mathbf{1}-\tilde{M}\omega\big\rVert_{2}^{2}:\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}\\
l\neq\mathbf{0}\end{subarray}}|\omega_{\alpha,l}|\leq V\Bigg\},

(82)

if we let
\gamma
be the
|J|
-dimensional vector indexed by
(\alpha,l)\in J
for which

\displaystyle\gamma_{\alpha,l}=\begin{cases}\big(\hat{\omega}^{d}_{n,V}\big)_{\alpha,l}&\mbox{if }(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}\\
0&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

then
(\hat{a}_{\mathbf{0}},\gamma)
is a solution to the original lasso problem (
13
).

Now, let
\check{M}
be the
n\times(1+|\tilde{J}|)
matrix with columns indexed with the set
\{\mathbf{0}\}\cup\tilde{J}
such that

\check{M}_{i,0}=1\qquad\text{for $i\in[n]$}

and

\check{M}_{i,(\alpha,l)}=\tilde{M}_{i,(\alpha,l)}\qquad\text{for $i\in[n]$ and $(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}$}.

Since

1+|\tilde{J}|=1+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(n_{k}-1)=\prod_{k=1}^{d}\big[1+(n_{k}-1)\big]=n=|I_{0}|,

\check{M}
is a square matrix.
If we concatenate
a_{0}
and
\omega
in the problem (
82
) and form a
(1+|\tilde{J}|)
-dimensional vector
\check{\omega}
indexed with the set
\{\mathbf{0}\}\cup\tilde{J}
, the problem (
82
) can be rewritten as

\underset{\check{\omega}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{1+|\tilde{J}|}}{\mathop{\rm argmin}}\Bigg\{\big\lVert y-\check{M}\check{\omega}\big\rVert_{2}^{2}:\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}\\
l\neq\mathbf{0}\end{subarray}}|\check{\omega}_{\alpha,l}|\leq V\Bigg\}.

(83)

Next, fix an
n
-dimensional vector
\theta
indexed with the set
I_{0}
and let
\check{\omega}
be the
(1+|\tilde{J}|)
-dimensional vector indexed with the set
\{\mathbf{0}\}\cup\tilde{J}
for which

\check{\omega}_{\mathbf{0}}=\theta_{\mathbf{0}}\mbox{\quad and \quad}\check{\omega}_{\alpha,l}=\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\cdot(H^{(2)}\theta)_{\tilde{l}+\alpha}\qquad\text{for $(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J},$}

where
\tilde{l}
is the
d
-dimensional vector with

\tilde{l}_{k}=\begin{cases}l_{k}&\mbox{if }k\in S(\alpha)\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then, by (
80
),

\displaystyle(\check{M}\check{\omega})_{i}=\theta_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\sum_{l\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:(n_{k}-2)]}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\Big(\frac{i_{k}}{n_{k}}-\frac{l_{k}}{n_{k}}\Big)_{+}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\cdot(H^{(2)}\theta)_{\tilde{l}+\alpha}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=(H^{(2)}\theta)_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\sum_{l\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}[n_{k}-1]}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(i_{k}-l_{k}+1)_{+}\bigg)\cdot(H^{(2)}\theta)_{\tilde{l}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=(H^{(2)}\theta)_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\sum_{l\in I_{0}^{(\alpha)}}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}\binom{i_{k}-l_{k}+\alpha_{k}}{\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i\geq l\}\cdot(H^{(2)}\theta)_{l}\bigg]=\theta_{i}

for each
i\in I_{0}
, and thus,
\check{M}\check{\omega}=\theta
.
From this result, we can also deduce that
\check{M}
is invertible.
Moreover, by (
E.6
), we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}\\
