l\neq\mathbf{0}\end{subarray}}|\check{\omega}_{\alpha,l}|=\sum_{\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\bigg[\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\cdot\sum_{i\in I_{0}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\alpha\}}\big|(H^{(2)}\theta)_{i}\big|\bigg]=V_{2}(\theta).

For these reasons, for the unique solution
\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}
to the problem (
43
), if we let
\check{\omega}
be the
(1+|\tilde{J}|)
-dimensional vector indexed with the set
\{\mathbf{0}\}\cup\tilde{J}
for which

\check{\omega}_{\mathbf{0}}=\big(\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}\big)_{\mathbf{0}}\mbox{\quad and \quad}\check{\omega}_{\alpha,l}=\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\cdot\big(H^{(2)}\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}\big)_{\tilde{l}+\alpha}\qquad\text{for $(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}$},

then
\check{\omega}
is the unique solution to the problem (
83
).
Due to the connection between the problem (
82
) and the problem (
83
), this implies that if

a_{\mathbf{0}}=\big(\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}\big)_{\mathbf{0}}

and
\omega
is the
|\tilde{J}|
-dimensional vector indexed by
(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}
for which

\omega_{\alpha,l}=\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\alpha_{k}}\bigg)\cdot\big(H^{(2)}\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}\big)_{\tilde{l}+\alpha}\qquad\text{for $(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}$},

then
(a_{0},\omega)
becomes the unique solution to the problem (
82
).
If we additionally let
\gamma
be the
|J|
-dimensional vector indexed by
(\alpha,l)\in J
for which

\displaystyle\gamma_{\alpha,l}=\begin{cases}\big(\prod_{k=1}^{d}n_{k}^{\alpha_{k}}\big)\cdot\big(H^{(2)}\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}\big)_{\tilde{l}+\alpha}&\mbox{if }(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}\\
0&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

the connection between the problem (
13
) and the problem (
82
) then shows that
(a_{0},\gamma)
is a solution to (
13
).
Consequently, by Proposition
2.2
, we can see that the function
f
on
[0,1]^{d}
defined by

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\big(\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}\big)_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{(\alpha,l)\in\tilde{J}}\big(H^{(2)}\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}\big)_{\tilde{l}+\alpha}\cdot\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\big(n_{k}x_{k}-l_{k}\big)_{+},

is a solution to the original optimization problem (
7
).
In addition, Proposition
2.2
also implies that for every solution
\hat{f}^{d,d}_{n,V}
to (
7
), the values of
\hat{f}^{d,d}_{n,V}
at the design points equal
\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}
, i.e.,

\hat{f}^{d,d}_{n,V}\Big(\Big(\frac{i_{k}}{n_{k}},k\in[d]\Big)\Big)=\big(\hat{\theta}^{d}_{n,V}\big)_{i}

for all
i\in I_{0}
.
∎

E.7
Proofs of Lemmas in Appendix
E.3

E.7.1
Proof of Lemma
E.6

Suppose
f=f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}\in S(V,t)
and let
a_{\alpha}=\nu_{\alpha}(\{\mathbf{0}\})
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
.
Then, we can write
f
as

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}a_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)})

for
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}
.
Note that

\displaystyle t^{2}\geq\|f\|_{n}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(x^{(i)})^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in I_{0}}f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[d]\big)\big)^{2}

where
I_{0}:=[0:(n_{1}-1)]\times\dots\times[0:(n_{d}-1)]
.
By the Cauchy inequality, we have

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}a_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}\bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)})\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}a_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}\bigg]^{2}-\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\,d|\nu_{\alpha}|(s^{(\alpha)})\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}a_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}\bigg]^{2}-V^{2}

for every
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d}
.
Using this inequality, we will bound
a_{\alpha}
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
first.
Observe that
|a_{\mathbf{0}}|=|f(\mathbf{0})|\leq\sqrt{n}t\leq V+\sqrt{n}t
.
The above inequality then implies that

\displaystyle nt^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in I_{0}}f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[d]\big)\big)^{2}\geq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1}\geq 0\\
i_{2},\dots,i_{d}=0\end{subarray}}f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[d]\big)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{n_{1}-1}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\big(a_{1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}+a_{\mathbf{0}}\big)^{2}-V^{2}\Big]=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{n_{1}-1}\big(a_{1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}+a_{\mathbf{0}}\big)^{2}-n_{1}V^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{n_{1}-1}\Big(\frac{1}{2}a_{1,0,\dots,0}^{2}\cdot\big(u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}\big)^{2}-a_{\mathbf{0}}^{2}\Big)-n_{1}V^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}a_{1,0,\dots,0}^{2}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{n_{1}-1}\big(u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}\big)^{2}-\frac{n_{1}}{2}(nt^{2})-n_{1}V^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}a_{1,0,\dots,0}^{2}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{n_{1}-1}{\rho}^{2}\Big(\frac{i_{1}}{n_{1}}\Big)^{2}-\frac{n_{1}}{2}(nt^{2})-n_{1}V^{2}

\displaystyle={\rho}^{2}\cdot\frac{(n_{1}-1)(2n_{1}-1)}{24n_{1}}\cdot a_{1,0,\dots,0}^{2}-\frac{n_{1}}{2}(nt^{2})-n_{1}V^{2}.

Here the last inequality follows from

u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}=u^{(1)}_{0}+\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{i_{1}}\big(u^{(1)}_{j_{1}}-u^{(1)}_{j_{1}-1}\big)\geq 0+\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{i_{1}}\frac{\rho}{n_{1}}=\rho\cdot\frac{i_{1}}{n_{1}}

for
i_{1}\in[n_{1}-1]
.
Thus, further applying the inequality
(x+y)^{1/2}\leq x^{1/2}+y^{1/2}
, we can obtain

|a_{1,0,\dots,0}|\leq C_{\rho}\cdot(V+\sqrt{n}t).

By the same argument, it can be shown that

|a_{\alpha}|\leq C_{\rho}\cdot(V+\sqrt{n}t)

for every
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|=1
.
Also, since

\displaystyle nt^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i\in I_{0}}f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[d]\big)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},i_{2}\geq 0\\
i_{3},\dots,i_{d}=0\end{subarray}}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\big(a_{1,1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}u^{(2)}_{i_{2}}+a_{1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}+a_{0,1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(2)}_{i_{2}}+a_{\mathbf{0}}\big)^{2}-V^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{n_{1}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{n_{2}-1}\big(a_{1,1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}u^{(2)}_{i_{2}}+a_{1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}+a_{0,1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(2)}_{i_{2}}+a_{\mathbf{0}}\big)^{2}-n_{1}n_{2}V^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{n_{1}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{n_{2}-1}\Big[\frac{1}{2}a_{1,1,0,\dots,0}^{2}\cdot\big(u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}u^{(2)}_{i_{2}})^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-\big(a_{1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}+a_{0,1,0,\dots,0}\cdot u^{(2)}_{i_{2}}+a_{\mathbf{0}}\big)^{2}\Big]-n_{1}n_{2}V^{2}

\displaystyle\geq{\rho}^{4}\Big[\frac{(n_{1}-1)(2n_{1}-1)}{12n_{1}}\cdot\frac{(n_{2}-1)(2n_{2}-1)}{12n_{2}}\Big]\cdot a_{1,1,0,\dots,0}^{2}-C_{\rho}n_{1}n_{2}(V^{2}+nt^{2}),

it follows that

|a_{1,1,0,\dots,0}|\leq C_{\rho}\cdot(V+\sqrt{n}t).

The same argument implies

|a_{\alpha}|\leq C_{\rho}\cdot(V+\sqrt{n}t)

for all
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|=2
.
Repeating this argument, we can show inductively that

|a_{\alpha}|\leq C_{\rho,s}\cdot(V+\sqrt{n}t):=\tilde{t}

for all
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
.

Now, fix
\delta>0
, which will be specified later, and let

{\mathcal{K}}=\big\{(k_{\alpha},\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\mbox{ and }|\alpha|\leq s):-(K+1)\leq k_{\alpha}\leq K\mbox{ for all }\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\mbox{ with }|\alpha|\leq s\big\},

where
K=\lfloor\tilde{t}/\delta\rfloor
.
Here
\lfloor\tilde{t}/\delta\rfloor
indicates the greatest integer less than or equal to
\tilde{t}/\delta
.
Also, for each
k\in{\mathcal{K}}
, let

M(k)=\{f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}\in S(V,t):k_{\alpha}\delta\leq a_{\alpha}\leq(k_{\alpha}+1)\delta\mbox{ for each }\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\mbox{ with }|\alpha|\leq s\}.

Then,

S(V,t)=\bigcup_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}M(k),

and thereby,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\log N(\epsilon,S(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{n})&\leq\log\Big(\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}N(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{n})\Big)\\
&\leq\log|{\mathcal{K}}|+\sup_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}\log N(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{n})\\
&\leq 2^{d}\log\Big(2+\frac{2\tilde{t}}{\delta}\Big)+\sup_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}\log N(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{n}).\end{split}

(84)

Fix
k\in{\mathcal{K}}
. Let
M_{\mathbf{0}}(k)
be the collection of all the constant functions on
[0,1]^{d}

(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\mapsto a_{\mathbf{0}}

where
k_{\mathbf{0}}\delta\leq a_{\mathbf{0}}\leq(k_{\mathbf{0}}+1)\delta
, and for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
, let
M_{\alpha}(k)
be the collection of the functions on
[0,1]^{d}
of the form

(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\mapsto a_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}+\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)}),

where
k_{\alpha}\delta\leq a_{\alpha}\leq(k_{\alpha}+1)\delta
and
\nu_{\alpha}
is a signed measure concentrated on
(\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\mathcal{U}_{k})\cap[0,1)^{|\alpha|}
with
|\nu_{\alpha}|([0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\})\leq V
.
Then, by definition,

M(k)\subseteq\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}M_{\alpha}(k),

which implies that

\log N(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\log N\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{2^{d}},M_{\alpha}(k),\|\cdot\|_{n}\Big).

(85)

Here for a collection of sets
\{X_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
\bigoplus_{i\in I}X_{i}
indicates the set
\{\sum_{i\in I}x_{i}:x_{i}\in X_{i}\mbox{ for }i\in I\}
.

Now, we will bound each term of the right-hand side of (
85
).
It is easy to show that

N(\epsilon,M_{\mathbf{0}}(k),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq 1+\frac{\delta_{\mathbf{0}}}{\epsilon}.

(86)

Hence, it suffices to bound
\log N(\epsilon,M_{\alpha}(k),\|\cdot\|_{n})
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
.
Fix
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
and let
\tilde{M}_{\alpha}(k)
be the collection of all the functions on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
of the form

(x_{j},j\in S(\alpha))\mapsto a_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}+\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)}),

where
a_{\alpha}
and
\nu_{\alpha}
satisfy the same conditions as in the definition of
M_{\alpha}(k)
.
With slight abusing of notation, let
\|\cdot\|_{n}
also denote the norm on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
defined by

\|f\|_{n}^{2}=\frac{1}{\prod\limits_{j\in S(\alpha)}n_{j}}\sum_{i\in\prod\limits_{j\in S(\alpha)}[0:(n_{j}-1)]}\Big(f\big(\big(u^{(j)}_{i_{j}},j\in S(\alpha)\big)\big)\Big)^{2}.

Then, clearly,

\log N(\epsilon,M_{\alpha}(k),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq\log N(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha}(k),\|\cdot\|_{n}).

(87)

Also, since
\tilde{M}_{\alpha}(k)
can be obtained by shifting
\tilde{M}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{0})
by some suitable function, we have

\log N(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha}(k),\|\cdot\|_{n})=\log N(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{0}),\|\cdot\|_{n}).

(88)

Moreover, if we let
\tilde{M}_{\alpha}
be the collection of all the functions on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
of the form

(x_{j},j\in S(\alpha))\mapsto\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)})

where
\nu_{\alpha}
is a signed measure concentrated on
(\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\mathcal{U}_{k})\cap[0,1)^{|\alpha|}
with
|\nu_{\alpha}|([0,1)^{|\alpha|})\leq V+\delta
, then

\log N(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{0}),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq\log N(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{n})

(89)

because
\tilde{M}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{0})\subseteq\tilde{M}_{\alpha}
.
For these reasons, we can see that it is enough to bound the metric entropy of
\tilde{M}_{\alpha}
in order to bound the metric entropy of
M_{\alpha}(k)
.

For
m\in[d]
and
S>0
, we denote by
\mathcal{G}_{m}(S)
the collection of the functions on
[0,1]^{m}
of the form

(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\mapsto\int_{[0,1)^{m}}(x_{1}-s_{1})_{+}\cdots(x_{m}-s_{m})_{+}\,d\nu(s)

where
\nu
is a signed measure concentrated on
(\prod_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{U}_{k})\cap[0,1)^{m}
with variation
|\nu|([0,1)^{m})\leq S
.
We can see that

\tilde{M}_{\alpha}=\mathcal{G}_{|\alpha|}(V+\delta)

after suitable re-indexing.
From the following lemma, which provides an upper bound of the metric entropy of
\mathcal{G}_{|\alpha|}(S)
, we can therefore obtain an upper bound of the metric entropy of
\tilde{M}_{\alpha}
.
We defer our proof of this lemma to Appendix
E.8.1
.

Lemma E.27
.

There exist positive constants
C_{\rho,m}
and
\kappa_{\rho,m}
depending on
\rho
and
m
such that for every
S>0
,

\log N(\epsilon,\mathcal{G}_{m}(S),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq C_{\rho,m}\Big(\frac{S}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{S}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{3(2m-1)}{4}}

provided that
0<\epsilon/S\leq\kappa_{\rho,m}
.
The log term can be omitted when
m=1
.

By Lemma
E.27
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\log N(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{n})&\leq C_{\rho,|\alpha|}\Big(\frac{V+\delta}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{V+\delta}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{3(2|\alpha|-1)}{4}}\\
&\leq C_{\rho,|\alpha|}\Big(\frac{2(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{2(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{3(2|\alpha|-1)}{4}}\end{split}

(90)

provided that
0<\epsilon\leq\kappa_{\rho,|\alpha|}(V+\delta):=\epsilon_{0}
.
Note that the logarithmic multiplicative factor can be omitted when
|\alpha|=1
. For
\epsilon_{0}<\epsilon\leq V+\delta
,

\log N(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq\log N(\epsilon_{0},\tilde{M}_{\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq\tilde{C}_{\rho,|\alpha|}\Big(\frac{2(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{2(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{3(2|\alpha|-1)}{4}}

for

\tilde{C}_{\rho,|\alpha|}=C_{\rho,|\alpha|}\Big(\frac{1}{\kappa_{\rho,|\alpha|}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big[\log\Big(\frac{2}{\kappa_{\rho,|\alpha|}}\Big)\Big]^{\frac{3(2|\alpha|-1)}{4}}\Big[2^{\frac{1}{2}}(\log 2)^{\frac{3(2|\alpha|-1)}{4}}\Big]^{-1},

where
C_{\rho,|\alpha|}
and
\kappa_{\rho,|\alpha|}
are the constants in (
90
).
Moreover, for
f\in\tilde{M}_{\alpha}
, we have

\|f\|_{n}\leq V+\delta,

which implies that
\log N(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{n})=0
for every
\epsilon>V+\delta
.
Combining those pieces, we can deduce that

\log N(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq C_{\rho,|\alpha|}\Big(1+\frac{2(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg[\log\Big(1+\frac{2(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{3(2|\alpha|-1)}{4}}

for every
\epsilon>0
.
This together with (
87
), (
88
), and (
89
) implies that

\displaystyle\log N(\epsilon,M_{\alpha}(k),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq C_{\rho,|\alpha|}\Big(1+\frac{2(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg[\log\Big(1+\frac{2(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{3(2|\alpha|-1)}{4}}

(91)

Note that we can omit the logarithmic multiplicative factor when
|\alpha|=1
.

By (
85
), (
86
), and (
91
), we have

\displaystyle\log N(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq\log\Big(1+\frac{2^{d}\delta}{\epsilon}\Big)+C_{\rho}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|=1\end{subarray}}\bigg[1+\frac{2^{d+1}(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+C_{\rho,d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
1<|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\bigg[1+\frac{2^{d+1}(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg[\log\Big(1+\frac{2^{d+1}(V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{3(2|\alpha|-1)}{4}}.

As a result, with the choice of
\delta=\epsilon
, (
84
) derives the conclusion

\displaystyle\log N(\epsilon,S(V,t),\|\cdot\|_{n})

\displaystyle\leq 2^{d}\log\Big(2+C_{\rho,s}\cdot\frac{V+\sqrt{n}t}{\epsilon}\Big)+C_{\rho,d}+C_{\rho,d}\Big(\frac{V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\rho,d}\Big(2^{d+1}+1+\frac{2^{d+1}V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg[\log\Big(2^{d+1}+1+\frac{2^{d+1}V}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{3(2s-1)}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{d}\log\Big(2+C_{\rho,s}\cdot\frac{V+\sqrt{n}t}{\epsilon}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{\rho,d}\Big(2^{d+1}+1+\frac{2^{d+1}V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg[\log\Big(2^{d+1}+1+\frac{2^{d+1}V}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{\frac{3(2s-1)}{4}}.

∎

E.7.2
Proof of Lemma
E.7

We basically follows the proof of
Fang, Guntuboyina and
Sen 2021
, Lemma C.5 here.
First, note that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{u}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big[\log\frac{u}{\epsilon}\Big]^{k}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle=u\int_{\tau}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{3}{4}v}v^{k}\,dv,

\displaystyle v=\log\frac{u}{\epsilon}

where
\tau=\log(u/t)
.
If
\tau\leq 1
,

\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{u}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big[\log\frac{u}{\epsilon}\Big]^{k}\,d\epsilon\leq u\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{3}{4}v}v^{k}\,dv\leq Cue^{-\frac{3}{4}\tau}=Cu^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}},

so the lemma directly follows.
Otherwise, applying integration by parts iteratively, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{3}{4}v}v^{k}\,dv

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3}e^{-\frac{3}{4}\tau}\tau^{k}+\frac{4}{3}k\int_{\tau}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{3}{4}v}v^{k-1}\,dv

\displaystyle=\dots=\frac{4}{3}e^{-\frac{3}{4}\tau}\tau^{k}+\dots+\Big(\frac{4}{3}\Big)^{\lfloor k\rfloor+1}k\cdots(k-\lfloor k\rfloor+1)e^{-\frac{3}{4}\tau}\tau^{k-\lfloor k\rfloor}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\Big(\frac{4}{3}\Big)^{\lfloor k\rfloor+1}k\cdots(k-\lfloor k\rfloor)\int_{\tau}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{3}{4}v}v^{k-\lfloor k\rfloor-1}\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}e^{-\frac{3}{4}\tau}\tau^{k}+C_{k}\int_{\tau}^{+\infty}e^{-\frac{3}{4}v}v^{k-\lfloor k\rfloor-1}\,dv

\displaystyle\leq C_{k}e^{-\frac{3}{4}\tau}\tau^{k}+C_{k}e^{-\frac{3}{4}\tau}=C_{k}u^{-\frac{3}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}(1+\tau^{k}).

Here
\lfloor k\rfloor
indicates the greatest integer less than or equal to
k
, and the inequalities are due to the fact that
\tau>1
.
From this result, we can derive the conclusion

\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{u}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big[\log\frac{u}{\epsilon}\Big]^{k}\,d\epsilon\leq C_{k}u^{\frac{1}{4}}t^{\frac{3}{4}}(1+\tau^{k}).

∎

E.7.3
Proof of Lemma
E.11

For
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
and
l\in\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:(N_{k}-1)]
, let
R_{l}^{(\alpha)}=\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}[l_{k}/N_{k},(l_{k}+1)/N_{k})
and define discrete signed measures
\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,l}
and
\mu_{\alpha}
as in the proof of Lemma
2.1
.
We will show that
f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\mu_{\alpha}\}}
constructed from these signed measures
\mu_{\alpha}
satisfies all the desired conditions.

First, as in the proof of Lemma
2.1
, it can be shown that

f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\mu_{\alpha}\}}\Big(\frac{i_{1}}{N_{1}},\dots,\frac{i_{d}}{N_{d}}\Big)=f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}\Big(\frac{i_{1}}{N_{1}},\dots,\frac{i_{d}}{N_{d}}\Big)

(92)

for every
i\in\prod_{k\in[d]}[0:N_{k}]
and

V_{\text{mars}}(f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\mu_{\alpha}\}})\leq V_{\text{mars}}(f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}).

Also, by repeating the arguments in the proof of Lemma
2.1
, we can show that

\int_{[0,1]^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,l}(t^{(\alpha)})=\int_{R_{l}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})

(93)

if
x_{k}\geq(l_{k}+1)/N_{k}
for all
k\in S(\alpha)
, and that

|\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,l}|([0,1]^{|\alpha|})\leq|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{l}^{(\alpha)}\big).

(94)

Fix
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1)^{d}
and suppose that
i_{k}/N_{k}\leq x_{k}<(i_{k}+1)/N_{k}
for
k\in[d]
.
We then have

\displaystyle\big|f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\mu_{\alpha}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})-f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{l\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:i_{k}]}\bigg|\int_{[0,1]^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,l}(t^{(\alpha)})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\int_{R_{l}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|.

If
l\in\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:(i_{k}-1)]
, then

\bigg|\int_{[0,1]^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,l}(t^{(\alpha)})-\int_{R_{l}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|=0

because of (
93
).
Otherwise, if
l\in\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:i_{k}]\setminus\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:(i_{k}-1)]
, there exists
j\in S(\alpha)
such that
l_{j}=i_{j}
.
Hence, by (
94
),

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{[0,1]^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,l}(t^{(\alpha)})-\int_{R_{l}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{N_{j}}\cdot\Big(|\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,l}|\big([0,1]^{|\alpha|}\big)+|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{l}^{(\alpha)}\big)\Big)\leq\frac{2}{N}\cdot|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{l}^{(\alpha)}\big)=\frac{2}{N}\cdot|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{l}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)

if
l\neq\mathbf{0}
, and

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{[0,1]^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,\mathbf{0}}(t^{(\alpha)})-\int_{R_{\mathbf{0}}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\bigg|\int_{[0,1]^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,\mathbf{0}}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|+\bigg|\int_{R_{\mathbf{0}}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\bigg|\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}x_{k}\cdot\big(|\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,\mathbf{0}}|(\{\mathbf{0}\})-|\nu_{\alpha}|(\{\mathbf{0}\})\big)\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{N_{j}}\cdot\Big(|\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,\mathbf{0}}|\big([0,1]^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)+|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{\mathbf{0}}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)+\big||\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,\mathbf{0}}|(\{\mathbf{0}\})-|\nu_{\alpha}|(\{\mathbf{0}\})\big|\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{N}\cdot\bigg(|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{\mathbf{0}}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\{0,1\}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\bigg|\int_{R^{(\alpha)}_{\mathbf{0}}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{1/N_{k}-t_{k}}{1/N_{k}-0}\bigg)^{1-\delta_{k}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{t_{k}-0}{1/N_{k}-0}\bigg)^{\delta_{k}}\bigg]\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\bigg|\int_{R^{(\alpha)}_{\mathbf{0}}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\bigg(\frac{1/N_{k}-t_{k}}{1/N_{k}-0}\bigg)\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{N}\cdot\bigg(|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{\mathbf{0}}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{\delta\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}\{0,1\}}\bigg|\int_{R^{(\alpha)}_{\mathbf{0}}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}\bigg[\bigg(\frac{1/N_{k}-t_{k}}{1/N_{k}-0}\bigg)^{1-\delta_{k}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{t_{k}-0}{1/N_{k}-0}\bigg)^{\delta_{k}}\bigg]\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2}{N}\cdot|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{\mathbf{0}}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big),

where the third inequality is from the definition of
\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,\mathbf{0}}
.
Using these results, we can first show that

\displaystyle\big|f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\mu_{\alpha}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})-f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{l\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:i_{k}]}\bigg|\int_{[0,1]^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,l}(t^{(\alpha)})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\int_{R_{l}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{l\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:i_{k}]}\frac{2}{N}\cdot|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{l}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2}{N}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}|\nu_{\alpha}|\big([0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)=\frac{2}{N}\cdot V_{\text{mars}}(f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}),

which directly leads to that

\|f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\mu_{\alpha}\}}-f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{N}\cdot V_{\text{mars}}(f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}).

Also, we can show that

\displaystyle\big|f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\mu_{\alpha}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})-f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{j\in S(\alpha)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:i_{k}]\\
l_{j}=i_{j}\end{subarray}}\bigg|\int_{[0,1]^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\tilde{\mu}_{\alpha,l}(t^{(\alpha)})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\int_{R_{l}^{(\alpha)}}\prod_{k\in S(\alpha)}(x_{k}-t_{k})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)})\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{j\in S(\alpha)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l\in\prod\limits_{k\in S(\alpha)}[0:(N_{k}-1)]\\
l_{j}=i_{j}\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{N}\cdot|\nu_{\alpha}|\big(R_{l}^{(\alpha)}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2}{N}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{j\in S(\alpha)}|\nu_{\alpha}|\bigg(\Big(\Big[\frac{i_{j}}{N_{j}},\frac{i_{j}+1}{N_{j}}\Big)\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in S(\alpha)\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}[0,1)\Big)\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\bigg).

From this result, we can derive that

\displaystyle\|f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}-f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\mu_{\alpha}\}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\big(f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\mu_{\alpha}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})-f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\big)^{2}p_{0}(x)dx

\displaystyle\quad\leq B\cdot\sum_{i\in\prod\limits_{k=1}^{d}[0:(N_{k}-1)]}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}\frac{1}{N_{k}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Bigg[\frac{2}{N}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{j\in S(\alpha)}|\nu_{\alpha}|\bigg(\Big(\Big[\frac{i_{j}}{N_{j}},\frac{i_{j}+1}{N_{j}}\Big)\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in S(\alpha)\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}[0,1)\Big)\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\bigg)\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{4B}{N^{2}}\cdot\sum_{i\in\prod\limits_{k=1}^{d}[0:(N_{k}-1)]}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}\frac{1}{N_{k}}\bigg)\cdot\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}|\alpha|\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{j\in S(\alpha)}\Bigg[|\nu_{\alpha}|\bigg(\Big(\Big[\frac{i_{j}}{N_{j}},\frac{i_{j}+1}{N_{j}}\Big)\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in S(\alpha)\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}[0,1)\Big)\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\bigg)\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C_{d}B}{N^{2}}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{j\in S(\alpha)}\sum_{i\in\prod\limits_{k=1}^{d}[0:(N_{k}-1)]}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{d}\frac{1}{N_{k}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Bigg[|\nu_{\alpha}|\bigg(\Big(\Big[\frac{i_{j}}{N_{j}},\frac{i_{j}+1}{N_{j}}\Big)\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in S(\alpha)\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}[0,1)\Big)\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\bigg)\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C_{d}B}{N^{3}}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{j\in S(\alpha)}\sum_{i_{j}=0}^{N_{j}-1}\Bigg[|\nu_{\alpha}|\bigg(\Big(\Big[\frac{i_{j}}{N_{j}},\frac{i_{j}+1}{N_{j}}\Big)\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in S(\alpha)\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}[0,1)\Big)\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\bigg)\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C_{d}B}{N^{3}}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\sum_{j\in S(\alpha)}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{j}=0}^{N_{j}-1}|\nu_{\alpha}|\bigg(\Big(\Big[\frac{i_{j}}{N_{j}},\frac{i_{j}+1}{N_{j}}\Big)\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in S(\alpha)\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}[0,1)\Big)\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\bigg)\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C_{d}B}{N^{3}}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}|\alpha|\cdot\Big[|\nu_{\alpha}|\big([0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{C_{d}B}{N^{3}}\cdot\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}|\nu_{\alpha}|\big([0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)\bigg]^{2}=\frac{C_{d}B}{N^{3}}\cdot\big(V_{\text{mars}}(f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}})\big)^{2},

where the second inequality is from the Cauchy inequality.
∎

E.7.4
Proof of Theorem
E.12

Here we almost follow the proof of
Han and Wellner 2019
, Proposition 2 and make a few modifications to the proof.
As in the proof of
Han and Wellner 2019
, Proposition 2, we also use the standard peeling argument along with the following moment inequality for empirical processes.

Lemma E.28
(
Giné, Latała and
Zinn 2000
, Proposition 3.1).

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a countable collection of real-valued functions defined on
\mathcal{X}
.
Suppose
X^{(1)},\dots,X^{(n)}
are i.i.d. random variables with law
P
on
\mathcal{X}
and
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}
are independent mean zero random variables independent of
X^{(1)},\dots,X^{(n)}
.
Then, there exists a universal positive constant
C
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|^{p}\bigg]\leq C^{p}\bigg[\bigg({\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\bigg)^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+p^{\frac{p}{2}}n^{\frac{p}{2}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\|f\|_{P,2}\Big)^{p}\cdot\max_{i}\|\xi_{i}\|_{2}^{p}+p^{p}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{p}\cdot\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}|f(X^{(i)})|^{p}\Big]\bigg]

for every
p\geq 1
.

Let
A
be the event where
\hat{f}
becomes a least squares estimator over
{\mathcal{F}}
.
By the assumption, we have
{\mathbb{P}}(A)\geq 1-\epsilon
.
Fix
r\geq 1
and let

{\mathcal{F}}_{j}=\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}:2^{j-1}rt_{n}<\|f-f_{0}\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\}

for each positive integer
j
.
Then, clearly,

\{f\in{\mathcal{F}}:\|f-f_{0}\|_{P,2}>rt_{n}\}=\biguplus_{j=1}^{\infty}{\mathcal{F}}_{j},

and thus

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathbb{P}}(\|\hat{f}-f_{0}\|_{P,2}>rt_{n})&\leq{\mathbb{P}}(A^{c})+{\mathbb{P}}(A\ \text{and}\ \|\hat{f}-f_{0}\|_{P,2}>rt_{n})\\
&\leq\epsilon+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}(A\ \text{and}\ \hat{f}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}).\end{split}

(95)

Recall that
\biguplus
indicates disjoint union.
Let
(\mathbb{M}_{n}(f):f\in{\mathcal{F}})
denote the stochastic process defined by

\mathbb{M}_{n}(f)=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}(f-f^{*})(X^{(i)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big((f-f^{*})(X^{(i)})\big)^{2}

and let
(M(f):f\in{\mathcal{F}})
denote the deterministic process defined by

M(f)={\mathbb{E}}[\mathbb{M}_{n}(f)]=-\|f-f^{*}\|_{P,2}^{2}.

Note that
\mathbb{M}_{n}(f)
can be alternatively represented as

\mathbb{M}_{n}(f)=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(y_{i}-f(X^{(i)})\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}.

Because
f_{0}\in{\mathcal{F}}
, if
\hat{f}
is a least squares estimator over
{\mathcal{F}}
, then it follows that

\mathbb{M}_{n}(\hat{f})-\mathbb{M}_{n}(f_{0})\geq 0.

Now, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}_{n}(f)-\mathbb{M}_{n}(f_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}(f-f_{0})(X^{(i)})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big((f-f_{0})(X^{(i)})\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f-f_{0})(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})

and that

\displaystyle M(f)-M(f_{0})

\displaystyle=-\|f-f^{*}\|_{P,2}^{2}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{P,2}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\|f-f_{0}\|_{P,2}^{2}-2{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim P}\big[(f-f_{0})(X)\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X)\big],

where
X
in the expectation is independent of all
X^{(i)}
.
By the assumption that
\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{P,2}\leq t_{n}/4
, we thus have that for every
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}

\displaystyle-(M(f)-M(f_{0}))

\displaystyle=\|f-f_{0}\|_{P,2}^{2}+2{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim P}\big[(f-f_{0})(X)\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X)\big]

\displaystyle\geq\|f-f_{0}\|_{P,2}\cdot\big(\|f-f_{0}\|_{P,2}-2\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{P,2}\big)

\displaystyle\geq 2^{j-1}rt_{n}\Big(2^{j-1}rt_{n}-\frac{t_{n}}{2}\Big)\geq 2^{2j-3}r^{2}t_{n}^{2},

where the first inequality is from the Cauchy inequality.
Hence, for each positive integer
j
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(A\ \text{and}\

\displaystyle\hat{f}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j})\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\big(\mathbb{M}_{n}(f)-\mathbb{M}_{n}(f_{0})\big)\geq 0\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\big((\mathbb{M}_{n}(f)-\mathbb{M}_{n}(f_{0}))-(M(f)-M(f_{0}))\big)\geq 2^{2j-3}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}(f-f_{0})(X^{(i)})\Big|\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big((f-f_{0})(X^{(i)})\big)^{2}-\|f-f_{0}\|_{P,2}^{2}\Big|\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(f-f_{0})(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim P}\big[(f-f_{0})(X)\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X)\big]\Big|\geq 2^{2j-6}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f(X^{(i)})^{2}-\|f\|_{P,2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)

(96)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim P}\big[f(X)\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X)\big]\Big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-6}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\bigg).

Here the second inequality is due to the fact that

-(M(f)-M(f_{0}))\geq 2^{2j-3}r^{2}t_{n}^{2}

for
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{j}
, and the third inequality is from the triangle inequality.

We first bound the first term of (
E.7.4
).
Fix a positive integer
j
.
By Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)\\
&\qquad\quad\leq\frac{1}{(2^{2j-5}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2})^{3}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\bigg].\end{split}

(97)

Also, by Lemma
E.28
(the moment inequality for empirical processes), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\bigg]\leq C\bigg({\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]\bigg)^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+C2^{3j}r^{3}t_{n}^{3}\cdot\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+Cn^{-\frac{3}{2}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{3}\cdot\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}|f(X^{(i)})|^{3}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C2^{3j}r^{3}n^{\frac{3}{2}}t_{n}^{6}+C2^{3j}r^{3}t_{n}^{3}\cdot\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+CM^{3}n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\big[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{3}\big].

Since
\xi_{i}
have finite
L^{q}
norm,

{\mathbb{E}}\big[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{3}\big]={\mathbb{E}}\big[\big(\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{q}\big)^{\frac{3}{q}}\big]\leq\Big({\mathbb{E}}\big[\max_{i}|\xi_{i}|^{q}\big]\Big)^{\frac{3}{q}}\leq\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}\big[|\xi_{i}|^{q}\big]\bigg)^{\frac{3}{q}}=n^{\frac{3}{q}}\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3},

where the first inequality is from Jensen’s inequality.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|^{3}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C2^{3j}r^{3}n^{\frac{3}{2}}t_{n}^{6}+C2^{3j}r^{3}t_{n}^{3}\cdot\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+CM^{3}n^{3(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{q})}\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3},

which together with (
97
) implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C\cdot\frac{1}{2^{3j}r^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{\frac{3}{2}}t_{n}^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3(1-\frac{1}{q})}t_{n}^{6}}.

Now, we bound the second term of (
E.7.4
).
We start by applying Markov’s inequality as in the case of the first term:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f(X^{(i)})^{2}-\|f\|_{P,2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)\\
&\qquad\quad\leq\frac{1}{(2^{2j-5}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2})^{3}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f(X^{(i)})^{2}-\|f\|_{P,2}^{2}\big)\Big|^{3}\bigg].\end{split}

(98)

Using the standard argument of symmetrization (see, e.g.,
van der Vaart and
Wellner 1996
, Lemma 2.3.1 or
Van de Geer 2016
, Theorem 16.1), we can show that

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f(X^{(i)})^{2}-\|f\|_{P,2}^{2}\big)\Big|^{3}\bigg]\leq 8{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})^{2}\Big|^{3}\bigg].

Also, by Lemma
E.28
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})^{2}\Big|^{3}\bigg]\leq C\bigg({\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})^{2}\Big|\bigg]\bigg)^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\|f^{2}\|_{P,2}\bigg)^{3}+Cn^{-\frac{3}{2}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}|f(X^{(i)})|^{6}\bigg].

Due to the contraction principle (see, e.g.,
van der Vaart and
Wellner 1996
, Proposition A.3.2),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})^{2}\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 4M\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{j+2}rM\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}.

Moreover, since
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M
for every
f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}
,

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\|f^{2}\|_{P,2}\leq M\cdot\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rMt_{n}.

Therefore, we have

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})^{2}\Big|^{3}\bigg]\leq C2^{3j}r^{3}M^{3}n^{\frac{3}{2}}t_{n}^{6}+C2^{3j}r^{3}M^{3}t_{n}^{3}+CM^{6}n^{-\frac{3}{2}},

which directly leads to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f(X^{(i)})^{2}-\|f\|_{P,2}^{2}\big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-5}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C\cdot\frac{M^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{\frac{3}{2}}t_{n}^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{6}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}},

because of (
98
).

The third term of (
E.7.4
) can be bounded in the same way as the second term.
Indeed, by repeating the same argument (except for the application of the contraction principle), we can show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}-\{f_{0}\}\\
\|f\|_{P,2}\leq 2^{j}rt_{n}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(f(X^{(i)})\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X^{(i)})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-{\mathbb{E}}_{X\sim P}\big[f(X)\cdot(f_{0}-f^{*})(X)\big]\Big)\Big|\geq 2^{2j-6}r^{2}\sqrt{n}t_{n}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C\cdot\frac{1}{2^{3j}r^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{\frac{3}{2}}t_{n}^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}\cdot\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}}.

As a result, by (
95
) and (
E.7.4
),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\|\hat{f}-f_{0}\|_{P,2}>rt_{n})\leq\epsilon

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\bigg[C\cdot\frac{1+M^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}+M^{3}}{2^{3j}r^{3}n^{\frac{3}{2}}t_{n}^{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3}n^{\frac{3}{q}}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}+M^{3})}{2^{6j}r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon

\displaystyle+C\cdot\frac{1+M^{3}}{r^{3}}+C\cdot\frac{\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{3}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}+M^{3}}{r^{3}n^{\frac{3}{2}}t_{n}^{3}}

\displaystyle+C\cdot\frac{M^{3}(\|\xi_{1}\|_{q}^{3}n^{\frac{3}{q}}+\|f_{0}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}+M^{3})}{r^{6}n^{3}t_{n}^{6}},

and replacing
r
with
t/t_{n}
completes the proof.
∎

E.7.5
Proof of Lemma
E.15

We first observe that every function
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}
can be uniquely split into a multi-affine term and the remaining term as follows.
Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be the collection of all the multi-affine functions

a(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}a_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j},

where
a_{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{R}}
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
,
and let
{\mathcal{H}}
be the collection of all the functions

h(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-t_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(t^{(\alpha)}),

where
\nu_{\alpha}
is a finite signed measure on
[0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
.
By the uniqueness of representation (see Lemma
E.1
), clearly, we have

\mathcal{F}_{\infty-\text{mars}}^{d,s}={\mathcal{A}}\oplus{\mathcal{H}},

where
\oplus
indicates direct sum.
Also, note that if
f=a+h
where
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
h\in{\mathcal{H}}
are in the forms above, then

\|h\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}|\nu_{\alpha}|([0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\})=V_{\text{mars}}(f).

Write
\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}=\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}+\hat{h}^{d,s}_{n,V}
and
f^{*}=a^{*}+h^{*}
where
\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V},a^{*}\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
\hat{h}^{d,s}_{n,V},h^{*}\in{\mathcal{H}}
.
Because

\|\hat{h}^{d,s}_{n,V}\|_{\infty}\leq V_{\text{mars}}\big(\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}\big)=V\ \text{ and }\ \|h^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq V_{\text{mars}}(f^{*})=V,

it follows that

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}-f^{*}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}-a^{*}\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{h}^{d,s}_{n,V}\|_{\infty}+\|h^{*}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}-a^{*}\|_{\infty}+2V.

Hence, in order to prove the lemma, it suffices to show that

\|\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}-a^{*}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(1).

Recall from (
7
) that there is no constraint on multi-affine terms in our definition of
\hat{f}^{d,s}_{n,V}
.
Thus, we can characterize
\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}&=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(y_{i}-\hat{h}^{d,s}_{n,V}(X^{(i)})-a(X^{(i)})\big)^{2}\bigg\}\\
&=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\xi_{i}-(\hat{h}^{d,s}_{n,V}-h^{*})(X^{(i)})-(a-a^{*})(X^{(i)})\big)^{2}\bigg\}.\end{split}

(99)

Let
\underline{X}
be the matrix whose rows and columns are indexed by
i\in[n]
and
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
, where
\underline{X}_{i\mathbf{0}}=1
and

\underline{X}_{i\alpha}=\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}X^{(i)}_{j}

for
\alpha\neq\mathbf{0}
.
Also, consider the coefficient vectors
\hat{\underline{a}}
and
\underline{a}^{*}
of the multi-affine functions
\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}
and
a^{*}
.
Specifically,

\hat{\underline{a}}=(\hat{a}_{\alpha},\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\text{ and }|\alpha|\leq s)

and

\underline{a}^{*}=(a^{*}_{\alpha},\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\text{ and }|\alpha|\leq s),

where

\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\hat{a}_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}

and

a^{*}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}a^{*}_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}.

Moreover, let
\hat{\underline{h}}
and
\underline{h}^{*}
be the
n
-dimensional vectors for which

\hat{\underline{h}}_{i}=\hat{h}^{d,s}_{n,V}(X^{(i)})\ \text{ and }\ \underline{h}^{*}_{i}=h^{*}(X^{(i)})

for
i\in[n]
.
Then, it follows from (
99
) that

\hat{\underline{a}}=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\underline{a}}\big\{\|\xi-(\hat{\underline{h}}-\underline{h}^{*})-\underline{X}(\underline{a}-\underline{a}^{*})\|_{2}^{2}:\underline{a}=(a_{\alpha},\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\text{ and }|\alpha|\leq s)\big\},

(100)

where
\xi:=(\xi_{i},i\in[n])
.

Now, let
\Pi_{\underline{X}}
be the projection operator onto the column space of
\underline{X}
.
Then, (
100
) implies that

\underline{X}(\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*})=\Pi_{\underline{X}}\big(\xi-(\hat{\underline{h}}-\underline{h}^{*})\big).

Since
\Pi_{\underline{X}}
is a projection operator,

\displaystyle\big\|\Pi_{\underline{X}}\big(\xi-(\hat{\underline{h}}-\underline{h}^{*})\big)\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi-(\hat{\underline{h}}-\underline{h}^{*})\|_{2}\leq\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sqrt{n}\big(\|\hat{h}^{d,s}_{n,V}\|_{\infty}+\|h^{*}\|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2V\cdot\sqrt{n}.

Also, we have that

\|\underline{X}(\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*})\|_{2}^{2}=(\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*})^{T}\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}(\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*})\geq\lambda_{\text{min}}(\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X})\cdot\|\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*}\|_{2}^{2},

where
\lambda_{\text{min}}(\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X})
is the smallest eigenvalue of
\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}
.
Hence, combining these results, we can derive that

\big(\lambda_{\text{min}}(\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}/n)\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\|\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2V.

(101)

Consider the symmetric matrix
\Sigma
whose rows and columns are indexed by
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
, where

\Sigma_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}={\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}X^{(1)}_{j}\cdot\prod_{j^{\prime}\in S(\alpha^{\prime})}X^{(1)}_{j^{\prime}}\bigg].

By the law of large numbers, for every
\epsilon>0
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}/n-\Sigma\|_{F}>\epsilon\big)=0,

where
\|\cdot\|_{F}
denotes the Frobenius norm.
Also,

\big|\lambda_{\text{min}}(\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}/n)-\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)\big|\leq\|\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}/n-\Sigma\|_{F}

due to Weyl’s inequality, and the following lemma, which we prove in Appendix
E.8.2
, ensures that
\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)>0
.

Lemma E.29
.

The smallest eigenvalue of
\Sigma
is positive, i.e.,
\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)>0
.

For these reasons,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\lambda_{\text{min}}(\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}/n)<\frac{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\big|\lambda_{\text{min}}(\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}/n)-\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)\big|>\frac{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\|\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}/n-\Sigma\|_{F}>\frac{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)}{2}\Big),

which leads to that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\lambda_{\text{min}}(\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}/n)<\frac{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)}{2}\Big)=0.

Consequently, by (
101
), we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*}\|_{2}>K\big)

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\lambda_{\text{min}}(\underline{X}^{T}\underline{X}/n)<\frac{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}^{2}>\Big\{\Big(\frac{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)}{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot K-2V\Big\}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\Big\{\Big(\frac{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)}{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot K-2V\Big\}^{-2}\cdot\|\xi_{1}\|_{2}^{2}

provided that
(\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Sigma)/2)^{1/2}\cdot K-2V>0
.
Here Markov’s inequality is used for the second inequality.
This result implies that for each
\epsilon>0
, there exists
K>0
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\big(\|\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*}\|_{2}>K\big)<\epsilon,

i.e.,
\|\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*}\|_{2}=O_{p}(1)
.
By the Cauchy inequality,

\|\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}-a^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*}\|_{1}\leq C_{d}\cdot\|\hat{\underline{a}}-\underline{a}^{*}\|_{2},

and thus, it follows that
\|\hat{a}^{d,s}_{n,V}-a^{*}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(1)
.
∎

E.7.6
Proof of Lemma
E.17

Our proof of Lemma
E.17
is based on the following maximal inequality for empirical processes involving the Bernstein norm.
For a random variable
X
with law
P
on
\mathcal{X}
and a real-valued function
f
on
\mathcal{X}
, the Bernstein norm
\|f\|_{P,B}
of
f
is defined as

\|f\|_{P,B}=\big(2{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\big[\exp(|f(X)|)-1-|f(X)|\big]\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

In fact, the Bernstein norm is not a norm because it is not homogeneous and does not satisfy the triangle inequality.
However, we can still use it for measuring the size of functions.

Lemma E.30
(
van der Vaart and
Wellner 1996
, Lemma 3.4.3).

Suppose
X^{(1)},\dots,X^{(k)}
are i.i.d. random variables with law
P
on
\mathcal{X}
, and
{\mathcal{F}}
is a countable collection of real-valued functions on
\mathcal{X}
.
Also, assume that
\|f\|_{P,B}\leq\delta
for every
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
.
Then, we have

{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\Big[\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\Big]\leq CJ_{[\ ]}(\delta,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B})\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[\ ]}(\delta,{\mathcal{F}},\|\cdot\|_{P,B})}{\delta^{2}\sqrt{k}}\bigg)

for some universal positive constant
C
.

Remark E.31
.

As in Theorem
E.12
, the countability assumption on
{\mathcal{F}}
in Lemma
E.30
is just for the measurability of the supremum.
This condition can be removed if
{\mathcal{F}}
is pointwise measurable;
that is,
{\mathcal{F}}
has a countable subset
{\mathcal{G}}
for which for every
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
, there exists a sequence
\{g_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}
in
{\mathcal{G}}
such that
g_{m}(x)\rightarrow f(x)
for every
x\in\mathcal{X}
.
In this case,

\sup_{f\in{\mathcal{F}}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}f(X^{(i)})\Big|=\sup_{g\in{\mathcal{G}}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}g(X^{(i)})\Big|,

so that the measurability issue can be avoided, and the lemma is still valid without the assumption of countability.

Let
P
be the law of
(X^{(i)},\epsilon_{i})
on
[0,1]^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}
.
Also, let
\mathcal{A}_{t}
be the collection of all the functions on
[0,1]^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}
of the form

\Phi_{f}(X,\epsilon)=\frac{1}{2M}\cdot\epsilon f(X)\qquad\text{ for $X\in[0,1]^{d}$ and $\epsilon\in{\mathbb{R}}$,}

where
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}
and
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t
, i.e.,
f\in B_{{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}}(t,0,\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}):=B(t,0,\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})
.
Since
B(t,0,\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})
is a subset of
C([0,1]^{d})
, and
(C([0,1]^{d}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
is a separable metric space,
B(t,0,\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})
is also separable with respect to the sup-norm.
Let
{\mathcal{G}}
be a countable dense subset of
B(t,0,\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})
with respect to the sup-norm.
For every
\Phi_{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{t}
, if we let
\{g_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}
be a sequence in
{\mathcal{G}}
for which
\|g_{m}-f\|_{\infty}\rightarrow 0
, then we have

\Phi_{g_{m}}(X,\epsilon)-\Phi_{f}(X,\epsilon)=\frac{1}{2M}\cdot\epsilon(g_{m}-f)(X)\rightarrow 0

for every
(X,\epsilon)\in[0,1]^{d}\times{\mathbb{R}}
.
Hence,
\mathcal{A}_{t}
is pointwise measurable, and Lemma
E.30
is valid for
\mathcal{A}_{t}
.

Note that for
\Phi_{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{t}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\Phi_{f}\|_{P,B}&=\bigg(2{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\Big[\exp\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{2M}\cdot\epsilon f(X)\Big|\Big)-1-\Big|\frac{1}{2M}\cdot\epsilon f(X)\Big|\Big]\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&=\bigg(2\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\Big[\Big(\frac{|f(X)|}{2M}\Big)^{m}\Big]\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leq\bigg(2\sum_{m=2}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\Big[\Big(\frac{|f(X)|}{2M}\Big)^{2}\Big]\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{c_{0}t}{2M},\end{split}

(102)

where
c_{0}:=(2(e-2))^{1/2}
.
Here the first inequality is due to that

\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M

for every
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}
, and the second inequality is from that
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t
.
Thus, by Lemma
E.30
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]=2M\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\Phi_{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Phi_{f}(X^{(i)},\epsilon_{i})\Big|\bigg]\\
&\qquad\quad\leq C\cdot 2M\cdot J_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{c_{0}t}{2M},\mathcal{A}_{t},\|\cdot\|_{P,B}\Big)\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[\ ]}(\frac{c_{0}t}{2M},\mathcal{A}_{t},\|\cdot\|_{P,B})}{(\frac{c_{0}t}{2M})^{2}\sqrt{k}}\bigg).\end{split}

(103)

Now, we relate the bracketing entropy of
\mathcal{A}_{t}
with respect to the Bernstein norm to that of
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}
with respect to the norm
\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}
.
Suppose
\Phi_{f}\in\mathcal{A}_{t}
and let
[f_{1},f_{2}]
be a bracket containing
f
.
Since
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M
, we can assume that
\|f_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq M
and
\|f_{2}\|_{\infty}\leq M
.
Also, let
p
and
q
denote the functions

p(\epsilon)=\epsilon_{+}=\max\{\epsilon,0\}\ \mbox{ and }\ q(\epsilon)=\epsilon_{-}=\max\{-\epsilon,0\}.

Then, clearly,

\Phi_{f}\in\Big[\frac{1}{2M}\{f_{1}\otimes p-f_{2}\otimes q\},\frac{1}{2M}\{f_{2}\otimes p-f_{1}\otimes q\}\Big].

Recall that
\otimes
denotes the tensor product between functions.
For example,
f_{1}\otimes p
indicates the function defined by

(f_{1}\otimes p)(X,\epsilon)=f_{1}(X)\cdot p(\epsilon)

for
X\in[0,1]^{d}
and
\epsilon\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Also, observe that by the same argument as in (
102
),

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{1}{2M}\{f_{2}\otimes p-f_{1}\otimes q\}-\frac{1}{2M}\{f_{1}\otimes p-f_{2}\otimes q\}\Big\|_{P,B}

\displaystyle\qquad=\Big\|\frac{1}{2M}\cdot(f_{2}-f_{1})\otimes(p+q)\Big\|_{P,B}

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigg(2{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\Big[\exp\Big(\Big|\frac{1}{2M}\cdot(f_{2}-f_{1})(X)\Big|\Big)-1-\Big|\frac{1}{2M}\cdot(f_{2}-f_{1})(X)\Big|\Big]\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{c_{0}}{2M}\cdot\|f_{2}-f_{1}\|_{p_{0},2}.

For these reasons, for every
\epsilon>0
, we have

N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{c_{0}\epsilon}{2M},\mathcal{A}_{t},\|\cdot\|_{P,B}\Big)\leq N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}),

which implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}J_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{c_{0}t}{2M},\mathcal{A}_{t},\|\cdot\|_{P,B}\Big)&=\int_{0}^{\frac{c_{0}t}{2M}}\sqrt{1+N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\mathcal{A}_{t},\|\cdot\|_{P,B})}\,d\epsilon\\
&=\frac{c_{0}}{2M}\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{1+N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{c_{0}\epsilon}{2M},\mathcal{A}_{t},\|\cdot\|_{P,B}\Big)}\,d\epsilon\\
&\leq\frac{c_{0}}{2M}\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{1+N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}\,d\epsilon\\
&=\frac{c_{0}}{2M}\cdot J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}).\end{split}

As a result, we can deduce from (
103
) that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}\\
\|f\|_{p_{0},2}\leq t\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\epsilon_{i}f(X^{(i)})\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\cdot J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\bigg(1+M\cdot\frac{J_{[\ ]}(t,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})}{t^{2}\sqrt{k}}\bigg).

∎

E.7.7
Proof of Lemma
E.18

Suppose
f=f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}
.
By the definition of
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)
, we have
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M
and
V_{\text{mars}}(f)\leq V+V_{\text{mars}}(f^{*})\leq 2V
.
Let
a_{\alpha}=\nu_{\alpha}(\{\mathbf{0}\})
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
.
Then, the function
f
can be written as

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=a_{\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}a_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)}).

As in the proof of Lemma
E.6
, we will bound
a_{\alpha}
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
first.

Note that
|a_{\mathbf{0}}|=|f(\mathbf{0})|\leq M
.
Also, since

f(1,0,\dots,0)=a_{\mathbf{0}}+a_{1,0,\dots,0}+\int_{(0,1)}(1-s_{1})_{+}\,d\nu_{1,0,\dots,0}(s_{1}),

we have

\displaystyle|a_{1,0,\dots,0}|

\displaystyle\leq|f(1,0,\dots,0)|+|a_{\mathbf{0}}|+\Big|\int_{(0,1)}(1-s_{1})_{+}\,d\nu_{1,0,\dots,0}(s_{1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq|f(1,0,\dots,0)|+|a_{\mathbf{0}}|+|\nu_{1,0,\dots,0}|((0,1))\leq 2M+2V.

By the same argument, we can show that

|a_{\alpha}|\leq 2M+2V

for every
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|=1
.
Moreover, since

\displaystyle f(1,1,0,\dots,0)=a_{\mathbf{0}}+a_{1,0,\dots,0}+a_{0,1,0,\dots,0}+a_{1,1,0,\dots,0}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\int_{(0,1)}(1-s_{1})_{+}\,d\nu_{1,0,\dots,0}(s_{1})+\int_{(0,1)}(1-s_{2})_{+}\,d\nu_{0,1,0,\dots,0}(s_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\int_{[0,1)^{2}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}(1-s_{1})_{+}(1-s_{2})_{+}\,d\nu_{1,1,0,\dots,0}(s_{1},s_{2}),

it follows that

\displaystyle|a_{1,1,0,\dots,0}|

\displaystyle\leq|f(1,1,0,\dots,0)|+|a_{\mathbf{0}}|+|a_{1,0,\dots,0}|+|a_{0,1,0,\dots,0}|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{(0,1)}(1-s_{1})_{+}\,d\nu_{1,0,\dots,0}(s_{1})\Big|+\Big|\int_{(0,1)}(1-s_{2})_{+}\,d\nu_{0,1,0,\dots,0}(s_{2})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{[0,1)^{2}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}(1-s_{1})_{+}(1-s_{2})_{+}\,d\nu_{1,1,0,\dots,0}(s_{1},s_{2})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq|f(1,1,0,\dots,0)|+|a_{\mathbf{0}}|+|a_{1,0,\dots,0}|+|a_{0,1,0,\dots,0}|

\displaystyle\quad+|\nu_{1,0,\dots,0}|((0,1))+|\nu_{0,1,0,\dots,0}|((0,1))+|\nu_{1,1,0,\dots,0}|\big([0,1)^{2}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq M+M+(2M+2V)+(2M+2V)+2V=6M+6V.

Similarly, it can be shown that

|a_{\alpha}|\leq 6M+6V

for all
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|=2
.
Repeating this argument, we can show inductively that

|a_{\alpha}|\leq C_{s}(M+V):=\tilde{M}

for every
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
.

Now, we repeat our arguments in the proof of Lemma
E.6
.
We cover
{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}
with function classes whose upper bounds of bracketing entropy can be obtained from those of
{\mathcal{D}}_{m}
for
m\geq 1
.
Fix
\delta>0
, which we will specify later, and let

{\mathcal{K}}=\big\{(k_{\alpha},\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\mbox{ and }|\alpha|\leq s):-(K+1)\leq k_{\alpha}\leq K\mbox{ for all }\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\mbox{ with }|\alpha|\leq s\big\}

where
K=\lfloor\tilde{M}/\delta\rfloor
.
For each
k\in{\mathcal{K}}
, we also let

M(k)=\{f_{a_{\mathbf{0}},\{\nu_{\alpha}\}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}:k_{\alpha}\delta\leq a_{\alpha}\leq(k_{\alpha}+1)\delta\mbox{ for each }{\alpha}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\mbox{ with }|\alpha|\leq s\}.

Then, by definition,

{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\}=\bigcup_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}M(k),

from which we can obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{F}}_{M}(V)-\{f^{*}\},\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})&\leq\log\Big(\sum_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\Big)\\
&\leq\log|{\mathcal{K}}|+\sup_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\\
&\leq 2^{d}\log\Big(2+\frac{2\tilde{M}}{\delta}\Big)+\sup_{k\in{\mathcal{K}}}\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}).\end{split}

(104)

Fix
k\in{\mathcal{K}}
.
Let
M_{\mathbf{0}}(k)
be the collection of all the constant functions on
[0,1]^{d}

(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\mapsto a_{\mathbf{0}}

where
k_{\mathbf{0}}\delta\leq a_{\mathbf{0}}\leq(k_{\mathbf{0}}+1)\delta
, and for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
, let
M_{\alpha}(k)
be the collection of all the functions on
[0,1]^{d}
of the form

(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\mapsto a_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}+\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)}),

where
k_{\alpha}\delta\leq a_{\alpha}\leq(k_{\alpha}+1)\delta
and
\nu_{\alpha}
is a signed measure on
[0,1)^{|\alpha|}
with
|\nu_{\alpha}|([0,1)^{|\alpha|}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\})\leq 2V
.
It then follows that

M(k)\subseteq\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}M_{\alpha}(k),

which leads to

\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{2^{d}},M_{\alpha}(k),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2}\Big).

(105)

It is clear that

N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,M_{\mathbf{0}}(k),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq 1+\frac{\delta_{\mathbf{0}}}{\epsilon}.

(106)

Hence, it is enough to bound
\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,M_{\alpha}(k),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})
for each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
.
For each
\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
with
|\alpha|\leq s
, let
\tilde{M}_{\alpha}
be the collection of all the functions on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
of the form

(x_{j},j\in S(\alpha))\mapsto\int_{[0,1)^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}(x_{j}-s_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\alpha}(s^{(\alpha)})

where
\nu_{\alpha}
is a signed measure on
[0,1)^{|\alpha|}
with variation
|\nu_{\alpha}|([0,1)^{|\alpha|})\leq 2V+\delta
.
As in the proof of Lemma
E.6
, we can easily show that

N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,M_{\alpha}(k),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{\tilde{p}_{0},2}),

(107)

where
\tilde{p}_{0}
is the probability density function on
[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
defined by

\tilde{p}_{0}(x^{(\alpha)})=\int_{[0,1]^{|\mathbf{1}-\alpha|}}p_{0}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})\,dx^{(\mathbf{1}-\alpha)}

for
x^{(\alpha)}\in[0,1]^{|\alpha|}
.
Since
p_{0}
is bounded by
B
,
\tilde{p}_{0}
is also bounded by
B
, i.e.,
\|\tilde{p}_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq B
.
Hence, from Theorem
3.6
, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,\tilde{M}_{\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{\tilde{p}_{0},2})&\leq\log N_{[\ ]}\Big(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{B}},\tilde{M}_{\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)\\
&\leq C_{|\alpha|}\Big(\frac{4\sqrt{B}(2V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big|\log\Big(\frac{4\sqrt{B}(2V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)\Big|^{2(|\alpha|-1)}.\end{split}

(108)

Combining (
105
), (
106
), (
107
), and
(
108
),
we can derive

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,M(k),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq\log\Big(1+\frac{2^{d}\delta}{\epsilon}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\bigg[\frac{2^{d+2}\sqrt{B}(2V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\bigg[\log\Big(\frac{2^{d+2}\sqrt{B}(2V+\delta)}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{2(|\alpha|-1)}.

With the choice of
\delta=\epsilon
, this together with (
104
) implies that

\displaystyle\log N_{[\ ]}(\epsilon,{\mathcal{F}}(V,M),\|\cdot\|_{p_{0},2})\leq 2^{d}\log\Big(2+C_{s}\cdot\frac{M+V}{\epsilon}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+C_{B,d}\Big(2^{d+2}+\frac{2^{d+3}V}{\epsilon}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg[\log\Big(2^{d+2}+\frac{2^{d+3}V}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg]^{2(s-1)}

as desired.
∎

E.7.8
Proof of Lemma
E.22

For each
\eta\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
, we have

\displaystyle|\nu_{\eta}|\big((0,1)^{s}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\int_{(0,1)^{s}}\Bigg|\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\eta_{p,i}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{s}\phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\bigg)\Bigg|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\Bigg(\int_{(0,1)^{s}}\bigg(\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\eta_{p,i}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{s}\phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\,dt\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\Bigg(\int_{(0,1)^{s}}\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\bigg(\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\eta_{p,i}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{s}\phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\bigg)\bigg)^{2}\,dt\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\Bigg(\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\int_{(0,1)^{s}}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{s}\phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\bigg)^{2}\,dt\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\Bigg(\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{s}\int_{0}^{1}(\phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j}))^{2}\,dt_{j}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\bigg(\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{s}2^{-p_{j}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}=V.

Here the inequality follows from the Cauchy inequality, the second equality is due to the fact that

\int_{0}^{1}\phi_{m,k}(x)\phi_{m^{\prime},k^{\prime}}(x)\,dx=0

for distinct
m
and
m^{\prime}
, and the third equality is from that
\phi_{m,k}\phi_{m,k^{\prime}}

\equiv 0
if
k
and
k^{\prime}
are different.
This directly implies that

V_{\text{mars}}(f_{\eta})\leq V
for every
\eta\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
.
∎

We first represent our function
f_{\eta}
in a simpler form.
For a positive integer
m
and
k\in[2^{m}]
, we let
\Phi_{m,k}
denote the real-valued function on
[0,1]
defined by

\Phi_{m,k}(x)=\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{u}\phi_{m,k}(t)\,dt\,du.

Then, it can be easily checked that
\Phi_{m,k}(x)=0
if
x\leq(k-1)2^{-m}
or
x\geq k2^{-m}
,
\Phi_{m,k}(x+2^{-m-1})=-\Phi_{m,k}(x)
for
x\in[(k-1)2^{-m},(k-1/2)2^{-m}]
, and
|\Phi_{m,k}(x)|\leq 2^{-2m-6}
for all
x\in[0,1]
.
Using these
\Phi_{m,k}
, we can represent
f_{\eta}
as

\displaystyle f_{\eta}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})

\displaystyle=\int_{(0,1)^{s}}\prod_{j=1}^{s}(x_{j}-t_{j})_{+}\,d\nu_{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\eta_{p,i}\int_{(0,1)^{s}}\prod_{j=1}^{s}\big((x_{j}-t_{j})_{+}\cdot\phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\big)\,dt

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\eta_{p,i}\prod_{j=1}^{s}\int_{0}^{1}(x_{j}-t_{j})_{+}\cdot\phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\,dt_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\eta_{p,i}\prod_{j=1}^{s}\int_{0}^{x_{j}}\int_{0}^{u_{j}}\phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(t_{j})\,dt_{j}\,du_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\eta_{p,i}\prod_{j=1}^{s}\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j})

for each
\eta\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
.

Now, we prove (
73
) using the above representation.
Suppose
H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})=1
for
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
.
Since
H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})=1
, there exists a unique
(p,i)\in Q
such that
\eta_{p,i}\neq\eta^{\prime}_{p,i}
, and thus

(f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}})(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}(\eta_{p,i}-\eta^{\prime}_{p,i})\prod_{j=1}^{s}\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j}).

Hence,

\displaystyle\|f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}=\frac{4V^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{s}\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j})\bigg)^{2}p_{0}(x)\,dx

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{4BV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{s}\int_{0}^{1}\big(\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j})\big)^{2}\,dx_{j}\leq\frac{4BV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{s}2^{-p_{j}}\cdot 2^{-4p_{j}-12}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{BV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-5l-12s+2}.

∎

Fix
\eta\neq\eta^{\prime}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
.
For each positive integer
m
and
k\in[2^{m}]
, let
h_{m,k}
be the real-valued function on
[0,1]
defined by

h_{m,k}(x)=\begin{cases}2^{\frac{m}{2}}&\mbox{if }(k-1)2^{-m}<x<\big(k-\frac{1}{2}\big)2^{-m}\\
-2^{\frac{m}{2}}&\mbox{if }\big(k-\frac{1}{2}\big)2^{-m}<x<k2^{-m}\\
0&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

and for each
(p,i)\in Q
, let
H_{p,i}
be the real-valued function on
[0,1]^{s}
defined by

H_{p,i}(x_{1},\dots,x_{s})=\prod_{j=1}^{s}h_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j}).

One can check that
\{H_{p,i}:(p,i)\in Q\}
is an orthonormal set with respect to the
L^{2}
inner product.
Also, let
g_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}
denote the function on
[0,1]^{s}
defined by

g_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{s})=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\big(\eta_{p,i}-\eta^{\prime}_{p,i}\big)\prod_{j=1}^{s}\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x_{j}).

Then, by Bessel’s inequality,

\|f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\geq b\|g_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\|_{2}^{2}\geq b\sum_{p^{\prime}\in P_{l}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in I_{p}}\langle g_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}},H_{p^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\rangle^{2},

(109)

where
\|\cdot\|_{2}
and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denote the
L^{2}
norm and the
L^{2}
inner product, respectively.
Note that for each
(p^{\prime},i^{\prime})\in Q
,

\langle g_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}},H_{p^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\rangle=\frac{V}{\sqrt{|P_{l}|}}\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}\big(\eta_{p,i}-\eta^{\prime}_{p,i}\big)\prod_{j=1}^{s}\langle\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}\rangle.

(110)

Now, we claim that for
(p,i),(p^{\prime},i^{\prime})\in Q
,

\prod_{j=1}^{s}\langle\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}\rangle=\begin{cases}2^{-\frac{5}{2}l-7s}&\mbox{if }(p,i)=(p^{\prime},i^{\prime})\\
0&\mbox{if }(p,i)\neq(p^{\prime},i^{\prime}).\end{cases}

(111)

We first assume
(p,i)\neq(p^{\prime},i^{\prime})
.
If
p\neq p^{\prime}
, then since
\sum_{j=1}^{s}p_{j}=l=\sum_{j=1}^{s}p^{\prime}_{j}
, there exists
j\in[s]
such that
p_{j}>p^{\prime}_{j}
, and thus
h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}
is constant on
((i_{j}-1)2^{-p_{j}},i_{j}2^{-p_{j}})
.
Also, recall that
\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x)=0
if
x\leq(i_{j}-1)2^{-p_{j}}
or
x\geq i_{j}2^{-p_{j}}
and
\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x+2^{-p_{j}-1})=-\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x)
for
x\in[(i_{j}-1)2^{-p_{j}},(i_{j}-1/2)2^{-p_{j}}]
.
For these reasons, we have
\langle\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}\rangle=0
.
Otherwise, if
p=p^{\prime}
, then
i
and
i^{\prime}
should be distinct.
Let
j\in[s]
be an index for which
i_{j}\neq i^{\prime}_{j}
.
Then,
\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x)h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}(x)=0
for all
x\in[0,1]
, which directly implies that
\langle\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p^{\prime}_{j},i^{\prime}_{j}}\rangle=0
.
Next, we assume
(p,i)=(p^{\prime},i^{\prime})
.
In this case, the claim follows from that

\langle\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}},h_{p_{j},i_{j}}\rangle=\int_{(i_{j}-1)2^{-p_{j}}}^{i_{j}2^{-p_{j}}}\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x)h_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x)\,dx=2^{\frac{p_{j}}{2}}\int_{(i_{j}-1)2^{-p_{j}}}^{i_{j}2^{-p_{j}}}|\Phi_{p_{j},i_{j}}(x)|\,dx=2^{-\frac{5}{2}p_{j}-7}

for each
j\in[s]
.

Combining (
109
), (
110
), and (
111
), we obtain

\|f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}\geq\frac{bV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-5l-14s}\sum_{p\in P_{l}}\sum_{i\in I_{p}}(\eta_{p,i}-\eta^{\prime}_{p,i})^{2}=\frac{bV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-5l-14s+2}\cdot H(\eta,\eta^{\prime}).

From this result, we can derive the conclusion

\min_{\eta\neq\eta^{\prime}}\frac{\|f_{\eta}-f_{\eta^{\prime}}\|_{p_{0},2}^{2}}{H(\eta,\eta^{\prime})}\geq\frac{bV^{2}}{|P_{l}|}\cdot 2^{-5l-14s+2}.

∎

E.8
Proofs of Lemmas in Appendix
E.7

E.8.1
Proof of Lemma
E.27

Recall that we define
{\mathcal{D}}_{m}
as the collection of all the functions
F
on
[0,1]^{m}
of the form

F(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=\int(x_{1}-s_{1})_{+}\cdots(x_{m}-s_{m})_{+}\,d\mu(s)=\int_{[\mathbf{0},x]}(x_{1}-s_{1})\cdots(x_{m}-s_{m})\,d\mu(s),

where
[\mathbf{0},x]=\{s\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}:0\leq s_{i}\leq x_{i}\mbox{ for all }i\in[m]\}
and
\mu
is a signed measure on
[0,1]^{m}
with variation
|\mu|([0,1]^{m})\leq 1
.
Lemma
E.27
is then a direct consequence of Theorem
3.2
and the following lemma, which connects the metric entropy of
\mathcal{G}_{m}(S)
and
{\mathcal{D}}_{m}
.
Here the covering number of
{\mathcal{D}}_{m}
in the lemma is under the
L^{2}
norm as in Theorem
3.2
.

Lemma E.32
.

There exists a positive constant
c_{\rho,m}
depending on
\rho
and
m
such that

N(\epsilon,\mathcal{G}_{m}(S),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq N\Big(\frac{c_{\rho,m}\epsilon}{S},{\mathcal{D}}_{m},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)

for every
S>0
and
\epsilon>0
.

Define a map
\Phi:\mathcal{G}_{m}(S)\rightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{m}
as follows.
Given
f\in\mathcal{G}_{m}(S)
of the form

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=\int_{[0,1)^{m}}(x_{1}-s_{1})_{+}\cdots(x_{m}-s_{m})_{+}\,d\nu(s)

where
\nu
is a signed measure concentrated on
(\prod_{k=1}^{m}\mathcal{U}_{k})\cap[0,1)^{m}
with variation
|\nu|([0,1)^{m})\leq S
,
we first consider the signed measure
\mu
on
[0,1]^{m}
satisfying the followings:

•

\mu
is concentrated on the set

\prod_{k=1}^{m}\Big\{u^{(k)}_{i_{k}}+\big(1-u^{(k)}_{n_{k}-1}\big):i_{k}\in[0:(n_{k}-1)]\Big\}.

•

For
(i_{1},\dots,i_{m})\in I_{0}:=[0:(n_{1}-1)]\times\dots\times[0:(n_{d}-1)]
,

\mu\Big(\Big\{\Big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}}+\big(1-u^{(k)}_{n_{k}-1}\big),k\in[m]\Big)\Big\}\Big)=\frac{1}{S}\cdot\nu\big(\big\{\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big\}\big).

Note that
|\mu|([0,1]^{m})\leq 1
since
|\nu|([0,1)^{m})\leq S
.
Next, let
F
denote the function on
[0,1]^{m}
of the form

F(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}(x_{1}-s_{1})_{+}\cdots(x_{m}-s_{m})_{+}\,d\mu(s)

and let
\Phi(f)=F
.
Clearly, for
(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\in[0,1]^{m}
,

F(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=0

if
x_{k}\leq 1-u^{(k)}_{n_{k}-1}
for some
k\in[m]
, and

F(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=\frac{1}{S}\cdot f\Big(\Big(x_{k}-\big(1-u^{(k)}_{n_{k}-1}\big),k\in[m]\Big)\Big)

otherwise.
Also, we can note that for

x=\Big((1-z_{k})\cdot u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1}+z_{k}\cdot u^{(k)}_{i_{k}}+\big(1-u^{(k)}_{n_{k}-1}\big),k\in[m]\Big),

where
i=(i_{1},\dots,i_{m})\in\prod_{k=1}^{m}[n_{k}-1]
and
0\leq z_{1},\dots,z_{m}<1
,

\displaystyle F(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{[\mathbf{0},x]}(x_{1}-s_{1})\cdots(x_{m}-s_{m})\,d\mu(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{j\in I_{0}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\Big(x_{k}-u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}-\big(1-u^{(k)}_{n_{k}-1}\big)\Big)\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i-\mathbf{1}\geq j\}\cdot\beta_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{j\in I_{0}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\Big[(1-z_{k})\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1}-u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\big)+z_{k}\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}}-u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\big)\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i-\mathbf{1}\geq j\}\cdot\beta_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{j\in I_{0}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[(1-z_{k})^{1-\delta_{k}}z_{k}^{\delta_{k}}\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}}-u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\big)\big]\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i-\mathbf{1}\geq j\}\cdot\beta_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[(1-z_{k})^{1-\delta_{k}}z_{k}^{\delta_{k}}\big]\cdot\sum_{j\in I_{0}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}}-u^{(k)}_{j_{k}}\big)\cdot\mathbf{1}\{i-\mathbf{1}\geq j\}\cdot\beta_{j}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[(1-z_{k})^{1-\delta_{k}}z_{k}^{\delta_{k}}\big]\cdot f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\bigg].

Here we let

\beta_{j}=\nu\big(\big\{\big(u^{(k)}_{j_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big\}\big)\qquad\text{for $j\in I_{0}$}

just for convenience.
Hence, for
f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{G}_{m}(S)
,

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi(f)-\Phi(\tilde{f})\big\|_{2}^{2}=\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\big|\Phi(f)(s)-\Phi(\tilde{f})(s)\big|^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{i\in\prod\limits_{k}[n_{k}-1]}\int_{u^{(1)}_{i_{1}-1}+(1-u^{(1)}_{n_{1}-1})}^{u^{(1)}_{i_{1}}+(1-u^{(1)}_{n_{1}-1})}\dots\int_{u^{(m)}_{i_{m}-1}+(1-u^{(m)}_{n_{m}-1})}^{u^{(m)}_{i_{m}}+(1-u^{(m)}_{n_{m}-1})}\big|\Phi(f)(s)-\Phi(\tilde{f})(s)\big|^{2}\,ds_{m}\cdots\,ds_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{S^{2}}\sum_{i\in\prod\limits_{k}[n_{k}-1]}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}}-u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\bigg[\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[(1-z_{k})^{1-\delta_{k}}z_{k}^{\delta_{k}}\big]\cdot\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\bigg]\bigg]^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{S^{2}}\cdot\frac{{\rho}^{m}}{n_{1}\cdots n_{m}}\sum_{i\in\prod\limits_{k}[n_{k}-1]}\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\bigg[\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[(1-z_{k})^{1-\delta_{k}}z_{k}^{\delta_{k}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\bigg]\bigg]^{2}\,dz,

where
\Delta f=f-\tilde{f}
.
For the last equality, we change the variables as

s_{k}=(1-z_{k})\cdot u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1}+z_{k}\cdot u^{(k)}_{i_{k}}+\big(1-u^{(k)}_{n_{k}-1}\big)

for
k\in[m]
.
For a fixed constant
0<r<1
, by the Cauchy inequality,

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]^{m}}\bigg[\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[(1-z_{k})^{1-\delta_{k}}z_{k}^{\delta_{k}}\big]\cdot\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\bigg]\bigg]^{2}\,dz
