\displaystyle\;\geq\int_{[r,1]^{m}}\bigg[\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[(1-z_{k})^{1-\delta_{k}}z_{k}^{\delta_{k}}\big]\cdot\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\bigg]\bigg]^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\;\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{[r,1]^{m}}z_{1}^{2}\cdots z_{m}^{2}\cdot\Big(\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\Big)^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\;\;-\int_{[r,1]^{m}}\bigg[\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}\}}\bigg[\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[(1-z_{k})^{1-\delta_{k}}z_{k}^{\delta_{k}}\big]\cdot\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\bigg]\bigg]^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\;\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{[r,1]^{m}}z_{1}^{2}\cdots z_{m}^{2}\cdot\Big(\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\Big)^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\;\;-(2^{m}-1)\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}\}}\int_{[r,1]^{m}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}\big[(1-z_{k})^{2(1-\delta_{k})}s_{k}^{2\delta_{k}}\big]\cdot\Big(\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\Big)^{2}\,dz

\displaystyle\;=\frac{(1-r^{3})^{m}}{2\cdot 3^{m}}\Big(\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\;-\frac{2^{m}-1}{3^{m}}\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}\}}\Big[(1-r^{3})^{\sum_{k}\delta_{k}}(1-r)^{3(m-\sum_{k}\delta_{k})}\Big(\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}-1+\delta_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\Big)^{2}\Big]

for all
(i_{1},\dots,i_{m})\in\prod_{k=1}^{m}[n_{k}-1]
.
Thus, for
f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{G}_{m}(S)
,

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi(f)-\Phi(\tilde{f})\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\bigg[\frac{(1-r^{3})^{m}}{2\cdot 3^{m}}-\frac{2^{m}-1}{3^{m}}\cdot\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}\}}\big[(1-r^{3})^{\sum_{k}\delta_{k}}(1-r)^{3(m-\sum_{k}\delta_{k})}\big]\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\frac{1}{S^{2}}\cdot\frac{{\rho}^{m}}{n_{1}\cdots n_{m}}\sum_{i\in\prod\limits_{k}[n_{k}-1]}\Big(\Delta f\big(\big(u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[m]\big)\big)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{(1-r^{3})^{m}}{2\cdot 3^{m}}-\frac{2^{m}-1}{3^{m}}\cdot\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}\}}\big[(1-r^{3})^{\sum_{k}\delta_{k}}(1-r)^{3(m-\sum_{k}\delta_{k})}\big]\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\frac{{\rho}^{m}}{S^{2}}\cdot\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{n}^{2}

since
f((u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[m]))=\tilde{f}((u^{(k)}_{i_{k}},k\in[m]))=0
if
i_{k}=0
for some
k\in[m]
.
Note that

\displaystyle\frac{(1-r^{3})^{m}}{2\cdot 3^{m}}-\frac{2^{m}-1}{3^{m}}\cdot\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}\}}\big[(1-r^{3})^{\sum_{k}\delta_{k}}(1-r)^{3(m-\sum_{k}\delta_{k})}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{(1-r)^{m}}{3^{m}}\cdot\bigg[\frac{1}{2}\cdot\Big(\frac{1-r^{3}}{1-r}\Big)^{m}-(2^{m}-1)(1-r)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot\sum_{\delta\in\{0,1\}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}\}}\Big[\Big(\frac{1-r^{3}}{1-r}\Big)^{\sum_{k}\delta_{k}}(1-r)^{2(m-1-\sum_{k}\delta_{k})}\Big]\bigg].

Therefore, by choosing
r
close enough to 1, we can show that there exists a positive constant
c_{\rho,m}
depending on
\rho
and
m
such that

\big\|\Phi(f)-\Phi(\tilde{f})\big\|_{2}\geq\frac{c_{\rho,m}}{S}\cdot\|f-\tilde{f}\|_{n}

(112)

for all
f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{G}_{m}(S)
.
Consequently,

N(\epsilon,\mathcal{G}_{m}(S),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq M(\epsilon,\mathcal{G}_{m}(S),\|\cdot\|_{n})\leq M\Big(\frac{c_{\rho,m}\epsilon}{S},{\mathcal{D}}_{m},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big)\leq N\Big(\frac{c_{\rho,m}\epsilon}{2S},{\mathcal{D}}_{m},\|\cdot\|_{2}\Big).

Here
M(\epsilon,X,\|\cdot\|_{n})
indicates the
\epsilon
-packing number of a set
X
under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{n}
, the second inequality follows from (
112
), and the first and the last inequality are from the general theory of packing numbers and covering numbers (see, e.g.,
Wainwright 2019
, Lemma 5.5).
∎

E.8.2
Proof of Lemma
E.29

Note that for every
v=(v_{\alpha},\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\text{ and }|\alpha|\leq s)
,

v^{T}\Sigma v={\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\Big(v_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}X^{(1)}_{j}\Big)\Big(v_{\alpha^{\prime}}\prod_{j^{\prime}\in S(\alpha^{\prime})}X^{(1)}_{j^{\prime}}\Big)\bigg]={\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}X^{(1)}_{j}\bigg)^{2}\Big]\geq 0.

Hence, it suffices to show that
v^{T}\Sigma v>0
for every
v\neq 0
in order to prove the lemma.
Suppose there exists
v=(v_{\alpha},\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\text{ and }|\alpha|\leq s)
such that
v^{T}\Sigma v=0
.
From the computation above, we can see that

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}X^{(1)}_{j}\Big)^{2}\Big]=0,

which directly implies

{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}v_{\alpha}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}X^{(1)}_{j}=0\bigg)=1.

(113)

For notational convenience, let
a_{d}
be the multi-affine function defined by

a_{d}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\{0,1\}^{d}\\
|\alpha|\leq s\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in S(\alpha)}x_{j}.

Then, we can restate (
113
) as

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{d}\big(X^{(1)}_{1},\dots,X^{(1)}_{d}\big)=0\Big)=1.

Now, observe that we can write
a_{d}
as

a_{d}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=b_{d-1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d-1})+a_{d-1}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d-1})\cdot x_{d}

(114)

for some multi-affine functions
a_{d-1}
and
b_{d-1}
of
x_{1},\dots,x_{d-1}
.
We can then show that the evaluations of
a_{d-1}
and
b_{d-1}
at
X^{(1)}
should also be zero almost surely.
More precisely, we can show that

{\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{d-1}\big(X^{(1)}_{1},\dots,X^{(1)}_{d-1}\big)=0\Big)=1\ \text{ and }\ {\mathbb{P}}\Big(b_{d-1}\big(X^{(1)}_{1},\dots,X^{(1)}_{d-1}\big)=0\Big)=1.

This is because

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{d}\big(X^{(1)}_{1},\dots,X^{(1)}_{d}\big)=0\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{d-1}\big(X^{(1)}_{1},\dots,X^{(1)}_{d-1}\big)=0\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{d-1}\big(X^{(1)}_{1},\dots,X^{(1)}_{d-1}\big)\neq 0\text{ and }X^{(1)}_{d}=-(b_{d-1}/a_{d-1})\big(X^{(1)}_{1},\dots,X^{(1)}_{d-1}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{d-1}\big(X^{(1)}_{1},\dots,X^{(1)}_{d-1}\big)=0\Big),

where the second equality is derived from the assumption that the
X^{(1)}
is a continuous random variable with a probability density function.

We can repeat this argument.
Suppose we have a multi-affine function of the first
i
variables
x_{1},\dots,x_{i}
whose evaluation at
X^{(1)}
is zero almost surely.
Then, we can represent it as in (
114
) with two multi-affine functions of one less variable,
x_{1},\dots,x_{i-1}
and show as before that the evaluations of those two functions at
X^{(1)}
are zero almost surely as well.
As the number of variables of multi-affine functions decreases by one at each iteration, we end up with constant functions that are zero almost surely, which means that they are identically zero.
If we recursively plug in them back to the recurrence relations between multi-affine functions, we can show that
a_{d}
is also identically zero, and this proves that the coefficient vector
v
of
a_{d}
is a zero vector.
∎
